⏺ I now have everything I need to answer this properly. This is a
  design-rationale question, so no code change — but the short answer is
  grounded in three things I just confirmed in your repo: src/extract.py (the
  call boundary), docs/TODO.md (the validation decision is explicitly tracked),
  and what Pydantic AI actually is per the docs you linked.

  Short answer

  Because Pydantic AI is an agent framework, not a validation library — and you
  don't need an agent here. The validation it would give you, you already get
  more directly from plain Pydantic + LiteLLM's Gemini hook. Adopting it would
  mean replacing your LLM call boundary to gain machinery you'd never use, which
  cuts against the project's stated minimalism rule.

  What validation actually does in this repo today

  Your extract() function (src/extract.py:91-129) already validates at three
  layers, and none of them needs Pydantic AI:

  1. Generation-time constraint — PatientLabels.model_json_schema() is passed to
  Gemini as response_schema, so the model is steered to emit conforming JSON in
  the first place (src/extract.py:64, 101-105).
  2. Gateway enforcement — "enforce_validation": True makes LiteLLM check the
  reply against that schema and raise litellm.JSONSchemaValidationError on a
  mismatch (caught in your HANDLED tuple, src/extract.py:43-48, 107-114).
  3. Typed materialisation — PatientLabels.model_validate_json(raw) parses the
  string into your typed object (src/extract.py:129). This line is the actual 
  validation that produces the prediction.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The strongest layer here is (1), generation-time constraint, which is
  qualitatively different from what an agent framework's retry loop does.
  Gemini's native response_schema shapes the decoding so invalid tokens are far
  less likely — that's "make it correct by construction." Pydantic AI's headline
  validation feature is the opposite shape: generate freely, validate after,
  and on failure feed the error back and re-call the model (its ModelRetry /
  reflection mechanism). For a single-shot batch extractor, constrain-then-parse
  is cheaper and more deterministic than generate-then-retry.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Why Pydantic AI specifically wasn't the tool

  1. It owns the call — you'd be re-platforming, not "adding a validator." Per
  the docs you linked, Pydantic AI's core abstraction is the Agent, which makes 
  the model call itself. You can't bolt it on purely to validate
  response.choices[0].message.content — that text is produced by
  litellm.completion(...), and Pydantic AI would want to be the thing issuing
  that call. Your CLAUDE.md has an explicit "one gateway" decision ("go through
  litellm.completion(...), not the raw SDK"); layering a second LLM-invocation
  framework over or instead of LiteLLM fragments that design.

  2. The validation is identical — Pydantic AI calls model_validate under the 
  hood too. Pydantic AI uses Pydantic for output validation. You're already
  calling Pydantic directly (model_validate_json). So Pydantic AI wouldn't add
  validation power; it would wrap the same call in an agent runtime. That's pure
  overhead for this use case.

  3. You'd inherit agent machinery you don't use. Tool calling, multi-turn
  message history, dependency injection, reflection/retry loops — none of that
  applies to "one transcript → one PatientLabels." This is a batch extractor,
  not an agent. Your CLAUDE.md is blunt about this: "the shortest, most minimal 
  code... No speculative abstraction and no scaffolding for features we don't 
  have yet." Pydantic AI is exactly that kind of scaffolding for this task.

  4. Telemetry is built around the LiteLLM response object. Your cost/latency
  logging reads response.usage and response._hidden_params["response_cost"]
  straight off the LiteLLM ModelResponse (src/extract.py:116-125), as documented
  in docs/TELEMETRY.md. Routing calls through Pydantic AI would mean
  re-deriving all of that through its result/usage API.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  There's a "right tool for the layer" point here. Your docs/TODO.md
  "Validation" section deliberately splits validation across three owners: the
  LiteLLM boundary (is it conforming JSON?), Pydantic (are the types/enums
  right?), and pandas (tabular sanity — nulls, dupes, enum membership on the
  aggregated frame). Pydantic AI only competes with the first two, and it
  competes by absorbing the whole call. It has no answer for the pandas-layer
  checks, which is where a lot of your real data-quality risk lives (e.g. the Q2
  (null)→unspecified and Q3 biologic-mislabel issues tracked in TODO).
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  When it would have been the right call

  To be fair to the option: Pydantic AI earns its weight the moment extraction
  stops being single-shot. If you moved to the README's "Next Steps" — e.g. an
  agent that calls tools (the branded-biologics registry lookup, a
  referral-pathway canonicaliser), does multi-step reasoning per transcript, or
  needs structured retries with self-correction across providers — then its
  agent runtime, typed dependency injection, and provider-agnostic model layer
  would start paying for themselves, and "one framework owns the call" becomes a
  feature rather than a cost. For the current MVP (constrain → parse →
  persist), it's heavier than the job.

  So the decision wasn't "Pydantic AI vs. validation" — it was "do we need an
  agent framework?" The answer for a deterministic batch extractor is no, and
  plain Pydantic gives you the same type-safety guarantee with three fewer
  layers of abstraction.

  ---
  Want me to capture this rationale in docs/TODO.md (under the "Validation —
  decide which layer owns what" section, which is currently an open decision) so
  the choice is documented for the reviewer? It's a natural fit there and would
  close one of the tracked open questions.

      43  
      44  - **Single-shot, batched — not multi-stage.** One system prompt
           extracts *all* `PatientLabels` fields in a single structured c
          all per patient; transcripts are then **batched** (10 per reque
          st) only to fit Gemini's free-tier ~20-requests/day cap — batch
          ing ≠ staging, it's still one pass over every field, just sever
          al patients per call. Single-shot minimises calls (decisive und
          er the rate cap), keeps the code simple, and lets the model set
           inter-dependent fields (`biologic_prescribed`/`biologic_taken`
          , `churn`, `referral_pathway`) with the whole story in view. Th
          e cost: every field competes for attention in one prompt, so th
          e hard fields suffer — the EDA shows `churn` unreliable and the
           GP node under-emitted. A multi-stage split (per-section prompt
          s run concurrently, with inspectable intermediates) is the #1 i
          tem in *Next Steps* below; it trades more calls + orchestration
           for better per-field accuracy.
      45  - **Uncertainty: absence vs. negation vs. truncation.** The pro
          mpt separates them by construction, onto the three schema mecha
          nisms above. A global rule — *"Leave a field null or empty when
           the transcript does not support a confident value"* — covers *
          *absence**. **Negation** rides on the prescribed-vs-taken boole
          ans + the `ReasonNotTaken` enum. **Truncation** gets the most e
          xplicit instruction: judge `churn` from how the transcript *end
          s*, treat a trailing "…" as a cut-off *only* if "…" isn't a rec
          urring stylistic device, prefer `false` when ambiguous, and — c
          ritically — *"keep this truncation signal distinct from a topic
           simply being absent … (that is 'not mentioned', not churn)."* 
          Honest limit: truncation is a lexical/structural property of th
          e transcript's end that the narrative under-determines, so the 
          instruction alone isn't enough — the gold audit found it unreli
          able in both directions (over-flagging and missed truncation), 
          and a deterministic tail-of-text rule would do better (`docs/TO
          _REVIEW.md`).

⏺ The current prompt is short and well-structured. Both fixes belong right next
  to the existing biologic guidance on line 14. Let me look at exactly what each
  fix must do:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  Q2 fights the global null rule on line 3 ("leave a field null when the
  transcript doesn't support a confident value"). For
  reasons_for_biologic_not_taken on a prescribed-but-not-taken patient
  (P047/P048), that rule produced null → the (null)→unspecified plot artefact.
  The fix must be a scoped override: for that one field+situation, never null —
  fall back to the UNKNOWN enum member the schema already defines.
  Q3 is the opposite — the line-14 rule is fine, the model just misapplied it:
  it tagged P046's taken biologic (Humira) before_biologic=True. A biologic is
  never "before a biologic", so the fix is to state that exclusion explicitly.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

          rney. Keep this truncation signal distinct from a topic simply

      12  complete story (that is "not mentioned", not churn).
      13

✻ Baked for 1m 50s

❯ what does before_biologic: false/true mean?                                   

⏺ It's a per-treatment boolean that lives on each entry in a patient's
  treatment_records list (src/schema.py:153-164) — not a patient-level flag. It
  marks where in the treatment timeline that one treatment sits relative to the
  patient's first biologic.

  - true — this treatment was tried before the patient's first biologic was
  started or offered. These are the earlier-line therapies (typically
  conventional/"step-up" drugs: aminosalicylates, corticosteroids,
  immunomodulators).
  - false — this treatment is not before a biologic: it's either the biologic
  itself, a later biologic switch, or anything tried after the first biologic.
  - null — undetermined (the schema allows bool | None).

  Why the field exists

  It's the before/after split that answers business Q3 — "what treatments are 
  tried before a biologic?" Crohn's care is usually a step-up ladder
  (conventional therapies first, biologics later when those fail), and Q3 asks
  what populates that earlier rung.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The schema docstring (src/schema.py:153-157) explains why the model sets this,
  rather than us computing it downstream: "the biologic may not appear as a
  treatment at all (e.g. prescribed-but-not-taken)." If you tried to infer
  "before vs after" in pandas by finding the biologic row and splitting the list
  around it, you'd fail on exactly the prescribed-not-taken patients — there's
  no biologic row to split on. Having the model stamp each record at extraction
  time, with the whole narrative in view, sidesteps that. The cost is that the
  model can mis-stamp it — which is precisely the P046 bug.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  The bug your fix targets

  Concretely, with the cases in play:

  ┌──────────────────────────┬────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┐
  │        Treatment         │      Correct       │         Reason          │
  │                          │  before_biologic   │                         │
  ├──────────────────────────┼────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
  │ Prednisone (tried first) │ true               │ conventional therapy,   │
  │                          │                    │ before any biologic     │
  ├──────────────────────────┼────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
  │ Humira — the biologic    │ false              │ a biologic is not       │
  │ the patient took (P046)  │                    │ "before a biologic"     │
  ├──────────────────────────┼────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
  │ Remicade → Humira switch │ both false         │ neither biologic is     │
  │  (P043)                  │                    │ before a biologic       │
  └──────────────────────────┴────────────────────┴─────────────────────────┘

  P046's Humira had been mislabelled true, which double-counts a biologic as if
  it were a pre-biologic treatment — inflating the Q3 "treatments before a
  biologic" tally. The prompt fix I added makes the exclusion explicit: "A 
  biologic is never 'before a biologic'… set before_biologic false for every 
  biologic entry itself." So once the running extraction lands, that Humira row
  should flip to false.

  (Background extraction is still retrying through the Gemini 503s — I'll
  confirm the P046/P043 rows once it completes.)

✻ Brewed for 26s

❯ what would null before_biologic mean?                                         

⏺ null means the model didn't commit to a before/after placement for that
  treatment record. It's the third state, distinct from both true and false.

  The schema makes null possible by typing the field before_biologic: bool | 
  None = None (src/schema.py:164), and there's no field-specific instruction 
  telling the model to always set it — so it falls under the prompt's global
  null rule (data/prompts/system.txt:3): "Leave a field null or empty when the 
  transcript does not support a confident value."

  In practice null arises in two quite different situations:

  1. Unclear ordering — the treatment is mentioned, but the transcript doesn't
  establish whether it came before or after the first biologic (no temporal
  cue).
  2. No biologic anchor exists — the patient never had a biologic
  prescribed/offered/mentioned at all, so "before a biologic" has nothing to be
  relative to. The split is undefined rather than unknown.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  This is a genuine gap, and it's worth seeing clearly: null here conflates 
  "unknown" with "not applicable." That cuts against this schema's stated design
  philosophy — the README's "three states, not one null" principle, which
  elsewhere goes to real lengths to separate absence / negation / truncation
  (e.g. the NOT_APPLICABLE vs NOT_MENTIONED vs null distinction on the reasons
  enums). before_biologic has no NOT_APPLICABLE equivalent because it's a bare
  bool | None, so the no-biologic case and the can't-tell case land on the same
  null.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Does it matter downstream? For Q3, no — the aggregation counts before_biologic
  == True, so any null (either flavour) is simply excluded, which is the
  correct outcome for both interpretations. So the analysis is safe; the
  semantic precision is what's lost.

  One thing the prompt fix I just added doesn't cover: for a patient with no 
  biologic at all, my new rule ("set true only for treatments before the first
  biologic… false for the biologic itself and anything after") technically
  implies everything should be false (there's no biologic, so nothing qualifies
  as "before" one) — but false wrongly signals "at-or-after the biologic." null
  is the more honest value there. If you want that case pinned down
  deterministically rather than left to the model's reading of the null rule,
  I'd add one clause:

  ▎ "If the patient never had a biologic prescribed, offered, or mentioned, 
  ▎ leave before_biologic null for every treatment — there is no biologic to 
  ▎ anchor the before/after split."

  Want me to fold that into system.txt before the extraction finishes, so it's
  part of this same run? (The background retry loop is still going — if I add it
  now it'll be picked up by whichever attempt next succeeds.)

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  There's a real lesson in how we hit it: the 503-retry storm consumed the day's
  request budget. Each 503 attempt (with LiteLLM's 2 retries) still counts
  against the ~20-requests/day free-tier cap the project assumes (docs/TODO.md)
  — so ~6 attempts × 3 requests ≈ the whole daily allowance, spent on failed
  calls. Next run we should retry 503s far more sparingly (fewer attempts,
  longer gaps), because on the free tier a failed request is as expensive as a
  successful one.

  The upside: we're blocked until reset anyway, so there's no rush — we can 
  finalise the schema design now and have the next run use the final shape.

  You're right that null is confusing

  tells you nothing about why.

  unclear." One token, two meanings.

  The honest fix is to stop using null as a carrier of meaning. There are two
  clean ways to do that, and since this changes src/schema.py, the prompt, and
  the Q3 EDA, it's genuinely your design call:

❯ What will this mean?  BiologicTiming enum                                     

⏺ Good moment to pause and make the meaning concrete before I write any code —
  especially since I just pulled the real data. Here's exactly what
  BiologicTiming will mean.

  It replaces the per-treatment field before_biologic: bool | None on each
  TreatmentRecord with biologic_timing: BiologicTiming — a single named-state
  value answering "where does this treatment sit relative to the patient's first
  biologic?"

  ┌─────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────┐
  │    Value    │             Meaning              │ From the data I just   │
  │             │                                  │       inspected        │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────┤
  │             │ tried before the first biologic  │ P046 prednisone &      │
  │ BEFORE      │ (the conventional/step-up        │ mesalamine; P001       │
  │             │ therapies)                       │ mesalamine &           │
  │             │                                  │ azathioprine           │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────┤
  │             │ the biologic itself, a switch,   │ P046 Humira; P001      │
  │ LATER       │ or anything after the first      │ Humira; P019 Humira    │
  │             │ biologic                         │                        │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────┤
  │             │ the patient never had a biologic │ (none above — all      │
  │ NO_BIOLOGIC │  — no anchor exists for          │ three have a biologic) │
  │             │ before/after                     │                        │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────┤
  │             │ a biologic exists, but this      │                        │
  │ UNKNOWN     │ treatment's ordering relative to │ (none above)           │
  │             │  it is unclear                   │                        │
  └─────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────┘

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The whole point of the enum is that it un-overloads the old null. Previously
  null had to carry both "no biologic exists" and "can't tell" — two genuinely
  different facts collapsed onto one valueless token. The enum splits them into
  NO_BIOLOGIC (a definite statement: there was no biologic) and UNKNOWN (an
  admission of uncertainty). And because it's a str, Enum like your other vocab
  fields, enforce_validation now checks membership at the model boundary —
  Gemini can't return an off-list value without the call being rejected, same
  guarantee you get on TreatmentOutcome/ReasonNotTaken.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  What changes downstream, concretely:
  - JSON becomes self-documenting — you'll read "biologic_timing": "LATER"
  instead of decoding "before_biologic": false.
  - Q3 stays the same answer — count records where biologic_timing == "BEFORE"
  replaces before_biologic == true. The BEFORE set = the old true set, so the Q3
  numbers don't move; only the code gets clearer.
  - The migration is exact for Q3: true → BEFORE, false → LATER, null → 
  NO_BIOLOGIC/UNKNOWN. Since only BEFORE is counted, the count is preserved
  precisely.

  One thing the data revealed that's worth flagging: P046's Humira is already
  before_biologic = False (→ LATER), not the True mislabel the TODO described.
  So either that case self-corrected in this run, or the TODO note predates this
  data. Either way, the Q3 "biologic counted as before-biologic" blemish may
  already be gone in the current artefacts — I'll confirm when I re-check Q3
  after the change.

  On naming: LATER means "at or after the first biologic" (it absorbs both the 
  biologic itself and everything after). If that reads as ambiguous to you, I
  can split it (IS_BIOLOGIC / AFTER) or rename to AT_OR_AFTER — but for Q3,
  which only cares about BEFORE, the single LATER bucket is the minimal choice.

  Want me to proceed with this exact four-value enum (with LATER as-is), or
  adjust the naming first?

      29 -- **Enums vs. free text vs. objects.** Closed vocabularies the 
         -analysis groups on are **enums** — `TreatmentOutcome`, `ReasonP
         -rescribed`, `ReasonNotTaken`, `ComorbidCondition`, `PathwayStep
         -` — so `value_counts`/joins get stable categories and membershi
         -p is checked at the model boundary (`enforce_validation`). Open
         -, high-cardinality, not-yet-canonical fields stay **free text**
         -: `biologic_type`, `TreatmentRecord.name`/`treatment_class` (a 
         -branded-drug + treatment-class registry is future work — `docs/
         -TODO.md`). Repeated, multi-attribute things are **structured ob
         -jects/lists**: `treatment_records` (a list of `TreatmentRecord`
         -, each carrying `before_biologic`, the flag that answers Q3), `
         -demographics`, and `referral_pathway` (`list[PathwayStep]`). Th
         -e visible cost of the free-text choice shows up in the **Q3 tab
         -le** — `conventional_therapy`/`conventional` and case variants 
         -are one class left un-normalised.

         +` — so `value_counts`/joins get stable categories and membershi

         + are one class left un-normalised.                             
      30  - **Three states, not one null.** "Not mentioned" (absence), "e
          xplicitly denied" (negation) and "cut off" (truncation) are kep
          t as *separate* signals rather than collapsed into a null: abse
          nce → `biologic_not_mentioned` + null/empty fields + `NOT_MENTI
          ONED`; negation → the `biologic_prescribed` vs. `biologic_taken
          ` pair + a specific `ReasonNotTaken` (`EXPLICIT_DENIAL`/`INSURA
          NCE_PROBLEMS`/…); truncation → `churn` + `JOURNEY_CUT_OFF`. A `
          NOT_APPLICABLE` member marks "genuinely doesn't apply" so it is
          n't confused with a blank "not yet annotated" cell. This is the
           split the churn table under Q4 operationalises.
      31  - **Evidence / auditability.** The model **does populate `evide
          nce_notes`** — a free-text rationale per patient (all 50 predic
          tions carry one, e.g. *"cycled through Remicade, Humira due to 
          loss of response; currently on Stelara; injection anxiety"*). W
          hat's **deferred is the manual side**: the gold annotation keep
          s `evidence_notes` as an unfilled column (`docs/SCHEMA.md`), an
          d structured **per-field** evidence — a snippet/turn-ref *per f
          ield* rather than one blob — is stretch task 2 (it costs tokens
           + prompt complexity). The broader audit trail is the **logged 
          raw model output** (`logs/extract.log` keeps the system prompt,
           schema, input and pre-validation response per call) plus the *
          *gold-set comparison** (`docs/TO_REVIEW.md`).
      32  - **Socio-demographics.** Deliberately minimal — `Demographics{
          gender, age}`, self-reported, grouped so a later "do pathways/o
          utcomes differ by demographic" cut is possible; everything else
           is noise for these four questions.

⏺ Update(README.qmd)
  ⎿  Added 1 line, removed 1 line
      43  
      44  - **Single-shot, batched — not multi-stage.** One system prompt
           extracts *all* `PatientLabels` fields in a single structured c
          all per patient; transcripts are then **batched** (10 per reque
          st) only to fit Gemini's free-tier ~20-requests/day cap — batch
          ing ≠ staging, it's still one pass over every field, just sever
          al patients per call. Single-shot minimises calls (decisive und
          er the rate cap), keeps the code simple, and lets the model set
           inter-dependent fields (`biologic_prescribed`/`biologic_taken`
          , `churn`, `referral_pathway`) with the whole story in view. Th
          e cost: every field competes for attention in one prompt, so th
          e hard fields suffer — the EDA shows `churn` unreliable and the
           GP node under-emitted. A multi-stage split (per-section prompt
          s run concurrently, with inspectable intermediates) is the #1 i
          tem in *Next Steps* below; it trades more calls + orchestration
           for better per-field accuracy.
      45  - **Uncertainty: absence vs. negation vs. truncation.** The pro
          mpt separates them by construction, onto the three schema mecha
          nisms above. A global rule — *"Leave a field null or empty when
           the transcript does not support a confident value"* — covers *
          *absence**. **Negation** rides on the prescribed-vs-taken boole
          ans + the `ReasonNotTaken` enum. **Truncation** gets the most e
          xplicit instruction: judge `churn` from how the transcript *end
          s*, treat a trailing "…" as a cut-off *only* if "…" isn't a rec
          urring stylistic device, prefer `false` when ambiguous, and — c
          ritically — *"keep this truncation signal distinct from a topic
           simply being absent … (that is 'not mentioned', not churn)."* 
          Honest limit: truncation is a lexical/structural property of th
          e transcript's end that the narrative under-determines, so the 
          instruction alone isn't enough — the gold audit found it unreli
          able in both directions (over-flagging and missed truncation), 
          and a deterministic tail-of-text rule would do better (`docs/TO
          _REVIEW.md`).
      46 -- **Determinism & reproducibility.** Three levers, in decreasin
         -g order of leverage. **(1) Structured-output mode** — `response
         -_format={"type": "json_object", "response_schema": PatientLabel
         -s.model_json_schema(), "enforce_validation": true}`: a Pydantic
         --derived schema constrains the *shape* and enum membership, and
         - LiteLLM validates the reply (raising `JSONSchemaValidationErro
         -r` on a mismatch), so format/parse variance is removed and down
         -stream pandas never meets an off-vocabulary value. **(2) Persis
         -ted predictions** — each chunk writes one validated JSON per pa
         -tient to `data/out/`, and the whole analysis (this README) read
         -s *only* those frozen artefacts with no API call, so every numb
         -er and plot is **exactly reproducible run-to-run** regardless o
         -f LLM nondeterminism; this is the real guarantee. **(3) Decodin
         -g params** — `temperature` is **pinned to `0`** (greedy) in `co
         -nfig.json` and imported by `extract.py` — extraction here is a 
         -**classification** task, so we want *same transcript → same lab
         -els*, not sampled variety. This build is the live test case for
         - *why*: a fresh re-run at the provider default (Gemini's defaul
         -t `temperature` is `1.0`) shifted long-tail outputs (the GP-nod
         -e count; which biologics got mislabelled `before_biologic`) whi
         -le the headline answers held — exactly the run-to-run drift `0`
         - removes. Temperature is **logged per run** (with the model) so
         - runs at different parameter levels stay comparable. A seed wou
         -ld add belt-and-braces, but with a reasoning-capable model `0` 
         -*minimises* rather than *guarantees* bit-identical output; **(2
         -)** is the hard reproducibility guarantee for the analysis. No 
         -explicit prompt **caching** yet — the static system prompt + sc
         -hema are identical across calls, so implicit context caching ma
         -y apply, and cached-token counts are logged (`docs/TELEMETRY.md
         -`).

      237 -> **Known extraction error (Q3) — error-handling note.** A `bi
          -ologic` appearing in "treatments before a biologic" carries `b
          -efore_biologic = true` on a biologic — which is either a genui
          -ne **biologic switch** (an earlier biologic tried before the o
          -ne ultimately taken) or a **model mislabel** (the *taken* biol
          -ogic itself flagged `before_biologic`, which can't be right — 
          -a treatment isn't "before" itself); the per-patient `treatment
          -_records` distinguish the two. So "treatments before a biologi
          -c" currently also counts biologics. We **surface this rather t
          -han silently drop it**: a clear include/exclude rule for biolo
          -gics, plus the prompt fix, is tracked in `docs/TODO.md`.      
      237 +> **Known extraction error (Q3) — error-handling note.** A `bi
          +ologic` appearing in "treatments before a biologic" carries `b
          +iologic_timing = BEFORE` on a biologic — which is either a gen
          +uine **biologic switch** (an earlier biologic tried before the
          + one ultimately taken) or a **model mislabel** (the *taken* bi
          +ologic itself flagged `BEFORE`, which can't be right — a treat
          +ment isn't "before" itself); the per-patient `treatment_record
          +s` distinguish the two. So "treatments before a biologic" curr
          +ently also counts biologics. We **surface this rather than sil
          +ently drop it**: a clear include/exclude rule for biologics, p
          +lus the prompt fix, is tracked in `docs/TODO.md`.

       83          _request_config_logged = True

       86 +def _with_503_retry(call: Callable[[], T]) -> T:              
       87 +    """Run a Gemini call with quota-aware retry. litellm's own
          + retries are OFF (``num_retries=0`` at                        
       88 +    the call site) so one failure costs ONE request, not three
          + — on the free-tier daily cap a failed                        
       89 +    request is as expensive as a successful one. ONLY 503 (``S
          +erviceUnavailableError``, transient                           
       90 +    overload) is retried, sparingly: up to ``RETRY_503_MAX`` t
          +imes with a long ``RETRY_503_DELAY_S``                        
       91 +    gap. Rate limits (429) and every other error are NOT retri
          +ed — they fail fast so the daily quota                        
       92 +    isn't spent on attempts that can't succeed."""            
       93 +    for attempt in range(RETRY_503_MAX + 1):                  
       94 +        try:                                                  
       95 +            return call()                                     
       96 +        except litellm.ServiceUnavailableError:               
       97 +            if attempt == RETRY_503_MAX:                      
       98 +                raise                                         
       99 +            logger.warning(                                   
      100 +                "503 ServiceUnavailable (attempt {}/{}) — slee
          +ping {}s before retry",                                       
      101 +                attempt + 1,                                  
      102 +                RETRY_503_MAX + 1,                            
      103 +                RETRY_503_DELAY_S,                            
      104 +            )                                                 
      105 +            time.sleep(RETRY_503_DELAY_S)                     
      106 +    raise AssertionError("unreachable")  # loop always returns
          + or raises                                                    
      107 +                                                              
      108 +                                                              
      109  # Log unexpected failures (traceback) + re-raise; retryable AP
           I errors handled below.
      110  @logger.catch(exclude=HANDLED, reraise=True)
      111  def extract(

⏺ That's the authoritative answer — straight from our installed LiteLLM. Let me

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Here's the precise answer, grounded in our installed LiteLLM (v1.89.1) source
  rather than the docs (whose hosted pages are thin on this).

  What num_retries does

  num_retries is LiteLLM's built-in automatic retry count for a failed
  completion() call (Tenacity-based, exponential backoff by default). It's
  passed via **kwargs — not an explicit parameter — and when you don't pass it,
  the effective default is 0 (no auto-retries).

  The key part is which errors it retries. That's governed by
  litellm._should_retry(status_code), whose actual implementation is:

  def _should_retry(status_code: int):
      """Retries on 408, 409, 429 and 500 errors."""
      if status_code == 408:  return True   # request timeout
      if status_code == 409:  return True   # lock timeout
      if status_code == 429:  return True   # rate limits   ← note this
      if status_code >= 500:  return True   # internal errors (incl. 503)
      return False

  So num_retries is a single blanket count applied across all of those error 
  classes at once — 408, 409, 429, and every 5xx including 503.

  Why I turned it off (num_retries=0)

  Because that blanket behaviour is exactly what burned your daily quota, in two
  ways:

  1. It multiplies failed requests. With the old num_retries=2, one failure
  became up to 3 requests (original + 2 retries). During the 503 storm, every
  attempt cost 3× against the ~20/day free-tier cap.
  2. It retries 429. _should_retry returns True for 429 — so LiteLLM was
  retrying your daily-quota error. That can never succeed once the day's budget
  is spent, and each retry still consumes a request slot, making the exhaustion
  worse.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The core mismatch: num_retries gives you one knob for all error types, but a
  free-tier daily quota needs opposite policies per type — retry 503 (transient,
  will clear) yet never retry 429 (terminal for the day). You can't express
  "retry 503 but not 429" with a single count. LiteLLM does have a per-exception
  RetryPolicy, but it's a Router-level feature, not available on the plain
  litellm.completion() path we use. So the cleanest way to get a differentiated
  policy is to switch LiteLLM's blanket retry off and own a tiny loop — which is
  what _with_503_retry does.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  So num_retries=0 hands retry control to our code:

  ┌─────────────────────┬───────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┐
  │                     │     LiteLLM       │                               │
  │        Error        │  num_retries=2    │        Our policy now         │
  │                     │     (before)      │                               │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ 503                 │ retried 2×        │ retried sparingly —           │
  │ ServiceUnavailable  │ (blanket)         │ retry_503_max=2,              │
  │                     │                   │ retry_503_delay_s=20          │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ 429 RateLimit       │ also retried 2×   │ not retried — fail fast       │
  │                     │ (wastes quota)    │                               │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┤
  │ every failure       │ up to 3 requests  │ 1 request each (+ only 503    │
  │                     │ each              │ adds controlled extra)        │
  └─────────────────────┴───────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┘

  One more thing worth noting: num_retries=0 is, in fact, LiteLLM's own default
  — I set it explicitly at both call sites (with a comment) so the intent is
  visible and nobody silently re-enables the blanket retry.

  ---
  That was the last open question. Status of the whole change set: all four
  tasks done — BiologicTiming enum landed, the quota-aware retry split
  implemented, docs (SCHEMA.md, DEBUGGING_ERROR_HANDLING.md, README retries
  paragraph) updated, and the gate is green (ruff/ruff format/ty all pass; the
  only failing test is the q3 one you accepted, which goes green once the
  post-reset run regenerates data/out with biologic_timing). Two things remain
  intentionally pending on that fresh run: the q3 test, and a full quarto render
  (I held it back so README.md isn't rebuilt with an empty Q3 against stale

  (transient, will clear) yet never retry 429 (terminal for the day). You can't 
  express "retry 503 but not 429" with a single count. LiteLLM does have a      
  per-exception RetryPolicy, but it's a Router-level feature, not available on  
  the plain litellm.completion() path we use. So the cleanest way to get a      
  differentiated policy is to switch LiteLLM's blanket retry off and own a tiny 
  loop — which is what _with_503_retry does.                                    

⏺ The right home is the "Retries are quota-sparing" paragraph I added to the ###
  Extraction section. Adding a tight one-sentence justification for why off
  right after the num_retries=0 claim:

⏺ Update(README.qmd)

      474  **Retries are quota-sparing.** On the free tier a *failed* req

      475  successful one, so retry policy is deliberately frugal and **t
           reats the two error classes
      476  differently**: litellm's own auto-retries are **off** (`num_re

⏺ I checked the Dagster code and your actual log — good news on the quota, and a
  clear answer on the retry question.

  What actually happened (from your log, this morning)

  2026-06-29 08:43:10  503 ServiceUnavailable (attempt 1/3) — sleeping 20s
  before retry
  2026-06-29 08:43:33  503 ServiceUnavailable (attempt 2/3) — sleeping 20s
  before retry
  2026-06-29 08:44:02  ServiceUnavailableError for batch [P001..P010] — gave up
  after retries

  So _with_503_retry did exactly its job: it tried, hit 503, retried twice with 
  20s gaps (~40s total), the demand spike didn't pass in that window, so it gave
  up and raised — that exception is the "I already retried and gave up" signal,
  not a "haven't tried yet."

  Cost of this failed run: 3 requests (1 initial + 2 retries), all on the first
  chunk. Not 20. You're fine.

  Will it retry after a break? No — not on its own.

  Two layers, both confirmed from src/pipeline.py:

  1. Our retry is exhausted — it already used its 2 attempts; it won't resume
  later.
  2. Dagster won't auto-retry either — your predictions asset is a plain
  @dg.asset with no RetryPolicy, so on failure Dagster just lights it red in the
  UI. It does not re-execute on a timer. Continuing requires you to click
  Materialize again.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  That missing RetryPolicy is actually the right default for your quota worry —
  and you should resist adding one. A Dagster RetryPolicy retries at the asset
  level, which would re-run the entire run_chunked over all 50 transcripts from
  the top. On a flaky-503 day that's a quota bonfire: every asset-retry restarts
  the whole batch. Keeping retries scoped to the single call inside
  _with_503_retry (3 requests, bounded) is far cheaper than retrying the whole
  stage.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  What to do (quota-safe)

  - Today's usage: ~3 of ~20. A clean full run costs 5 requests (5 chunks
  of 10). So you have room for a couple of attempts — but each chunk-1-failure
  burns ~3, so don't hammer it.
  - Wait for the spike to pass, then re-Materialize. 503 = "high demand, usually
  temporary" — but our 40s of retries didn't outlast this one. Re-running right
  now will likely 503 again and waste another ~3 requests. Give it a while (the
  spikes are often minutes), then try.
  - One caveat for mid-run failures: the Dagster predictions asset always
  re-runs all 50 — it doesn't resume from the last good chunk. This morning's
  failure was on chunk 1, so nothing was persisted and you've lost no progress.
  But if a later chunk fails next time, the CLI resumes cheaply where Dagster
  can't: uv run python src/main.py --offset=N skips the chunks already written
  to data/out/.

  If you'd rather a single run ride out longer spikes instead of failing and
  re-running, I can bump retry_503_delay_s (e.g. 20 → 60) and/or retry_503_max
  in config.json — but every extra retry is still one more request against the
  cap, so given your priority I'd leave it frugal and just re-run when the 
  model's less busy. Want me to do anything, or leave it for you to
  re-Materialize later?

✻ Crunched for 1m 57s

❯ it's a 503:  '503 Service Unavailable'                                        

⏺ Right — and that distinction matters, because a 503 is not a quota error. Let
  me separate the two cleanly, since it changes what's actually blocking you:

  ┌─────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────┐
  │             │     503 Service Unavailable      │     429 Rate limit     │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────┤
  │ Whose       │ Google's — the model is          │ Yours — you've hit     │
  │ problem     │ overloaded right now             │ your cap               │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────┤
  │ Meaning     │ "high demand, try later"         │ daily/minute quota     │
  │             │ (transient)                      │ exhausted              │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────┤
  │ Our         │ retried sparingly, then gives up │ not retried — fails    │
  │ handling    │                                  │ fast                   │
  └─────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────┘

  So right now you are not out of quota — the blocker is Google's capacity, not
  your 20/day. If you'd run out, you'd have seen 429 RateLimitError, not this.

  The catch: a 503 still appears to consume a request

  This is the part to be careful about. From our earlier session (2026-06-25), a
  run made ~18 rapid requests that all returned 503, and then started returning
  429 — i.e. the 503-failing requests had eaten the daily allowance before the
  quota error appeared. The requests were spread at ~3/min (well under the
  per-minute limit), so that points to the daily cap, meaning failed 503 
  attempts count against your 20/day.

  I can't prove it from Google's docs (they don't spell out whether 5xx-rejected
  requests are billed against RPD), so treat it as the safe assumption: a 503 
  costs you a request even though it failed.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  That assumption is exactly why the retry policy is frugal. If 503s were free,
  you'd retry aggressively to ride out any spike. Because they're (probably) not
  free, every retry is a real cost — so the design retries only twice, then
  stops and hands the decision back to you rather than burning your day chasing
  an overloaded model.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Practical takeaway

  - You've spent ~3 requests today (this morning's 1 + 2 retries). A clean full
  run needs 5. You have plenty left.
  - Don't retry-spam the 503 — each attempt likely costs ~3 more. Wait for the
  demand spike to pass (often just minutes), then re-Materialize once.
  - Nothing will retry on its own (our retry is spent; Dagster has no auto-retry
  policy), so it's entirely your call when to re-run — which is what you want
  for protecting the cap.

  So: it's Google being busy, not you being capped — just give it a bit and
  re-run, rather than hammering it now.

  So a limited CLI run starts from the top

  The CLI is the better tool here than Dagster for exactly your quota reason: it
  persists each chunk as it validates and lets you resume, so a later 503
  doesn't cost you the chunks already done. (Dagster's predictions asset re-runs
  all 50 every time — no resume.)

  Each --limit=10 is one chunk = one API request:

  uv run python src/main.py --limit=10 --offset=0    # P001–P010   (1 call)
  uv run python src/main.py --limit=10 --offset=10   # P011–P020   (1 call)
  uv run python src/main.py --limit=10 --offset=20   # P021–P030   (1 call)
  uv run python src/main.py --limit=10 --offset=30   # P031–P040   (1 call)
  uv run python src/main.py --limit=10 --offset=40   # P041–P050   (1 call)

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  Because each chunk writes data/out/json/P0xx.json only after it validates, the
  --offset you resume from is simply 10 × (chunks already done). You can verify
  progress anytime by re-running the schema check above — every patient that
  flips from OLD → NEW is durable and never needs re-spending. This is the
  resume capability Dagster lacks: a mid-batch 503 there forces all 50 to
  re-run.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Quota math

  - Spent today: ~3. Cap: ~20. A complete regeneration = 5 successful calls.
  - Doing them one chunk at a time costs the same 5 total if they all succeed —
  the only difference is granularity of resume on a 503.

  Since the model is currently 503-ing, I'd hold until the demand spike passes,
  then run. Want me to fire the first chunk now as a probe (--offset=0, ~1–3
  requests) to test whether the model's available — or leave it for you to run
  when you think it's quieted down?

❯ oh so no data was persisted?                                                  

⏺ Correct — no new data was persisted this run. But to be precise about what
  that means:

  - Nothing new was written — the schema check confirms 0 files have
  biologic_timing.
  - Nothing was lost or corrupted either — your existing 50 JSONs (old
  before_biologic schema, from the prior run) are sitting in data/out/json/
  exactly as they were. The failed run didn't touch them.

  Why it's all-or-nothing per chunk:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  In extract_batch, the write happens only after a successful response validates
  — the order is: call the model → parse → model_validate_json → save(). The
  503 fires on the call itself, several steps before save() is ever reached. So
  a 503'd chunk writes nothing — no half-finished or partial JSON. And because
  it died on chunk 1, the loop never advanced to chunks 2–5. Clean failure, zero
  side effects.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  So the state is simply: old-schema data still present, new-schema regeneration
  not yet started. When the model's available, a run (CLI chunks or a clean
  Dagster materialize) will overwrite those 50 with biologic_timing data, and
  then the q3 test goes green and the README can re-render.

  Nothing to clean up — you're at a clean starting line whenever you want to
  retry.

❯ so how long should i leave before re-trying dagster? i might as well just     
  re-run dagster pipeline for whole run given nothing was persisted             

⏺ There's no published number for it, but here's how to think about it
  practically.

  How long to wait

  A 503 "high demand" spike on Gemini typically clears in minutes, not hours.
  Your retries spanned only ~40 seconds, which wasn't enough to outlast this
  one. So:

  - First retry: give it ~20–30 minutes. That comfortably clears a typical
  spike.
  - If it 503s again on that retry, that signals a heavier/longer spike — back
  off to 1–2 hours (or just try a different part of the day).
  - There's no downside to waiting. The cap is daily and you've spent ~3 of ~20,
  so you have all day. Waiting for a quiet window is the cheapest strategy
  because the run is most likely to succeed first-try — no wasted retry
  requests.

  Re-running the whole Dagster pipeline — yes, fine

  You're right: since nothing persisted, a full re-materialize loses no
  progress. Go for it.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The one scenario where Dagster's lack of resume actually bites is this re-run 
  failing on a later chunk. Concretely: if it does chunk 1 ✓, chunk 2 ✓, then
  503s on chunk 3, it has persisted P001–P020 (new schema) — but a Dagster
  re-materialize redoes them from scratch, re-spending those 2 requests. The
  CLI's --offset=20 would skip them. So Dagster-full is the right call now
  (clean slate), but if a re-run dies partway, finish the remainder with the CLI
  rather than re-materializing the whole graph.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Cheapest way to know the spike has passed

  If you don't want to gamble the full run, fire one CLI chunk as a probe first:

  uv run python src/main.py --limit=10 --offset=0   # 1 request

  - Succeeds → spike's gone; either let that continue (4 more chunks via
  --offset) or just hit Materialize in Dagster.
  - 503s → still busy; wait longer. You've only risked ~1–3 requests instead of
  committing the whole run.

  So: wait ~20–30 min, then either probe with one CLI chunk or just
  re-Materialize the full pipeline — both are reasonable, and neither risks more
  than a few requests if the model's still busy.

  Want me to run that one-chunk probe for you in ~25 minutes (I can schedule
  it), or will you trigger the re-run yourself?

⏺ Here's everything from this batch, including the two diagnoses you asked for.

  1. splits.py removed + to_review corrected

  splits.py was referenced only in schema.py (×2) — both gone. The
  "validation/holdout split" framing is corrected to "human-reviewed set" across
  schema.py, post_extraction_eda.py, SYSTEM_DESIGN.{qmd,md}, TODO.md,
  README.qmd (×3), TO_REVIEW.md, and a test docstring. (I also fixed the stale
  _v4.ods → _v5.ods in the two schema.py docstrings I was already rewriting.)

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  "Split" was a genuine category error here, not just wording: it implies an ML
  train/test partition, but there's no training in this pipeline — to_review
  simply flags which transcripts a human gold-labelled. The accurate noun is a
  set (the reviewed cases evaluation scores against), which is exactly what
  gold_eval does: gold.loc[gold["to_review"] == 1].
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  2. treatment_class lowercased (Q3 dedup)

  Fixed at the source — q3_before_biologic now lowercases:
  (t.get("treatment_class") or t.get("name")).lower(). That collapses
  5-ASA/5-asa and Corticosteroid/corticosteroid into one row each. It's the
  right layer (deterministic normalization at aggregation), since model casing
  will always drift. (Takes visible effect on the next render; the count itself 
  is unchanged, just merged rows.)

  3. Churn table diagnosis (the 0 / 13 / 0)

  The table code is correct — the zeros are real model outputs, not a bug. From
  the live data:

  ┌──────────────────┬───────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │      State       │ Count │                     Why                      │
  ├──────────────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ biologic not     │       │ the model marks biologic_prescribed = True   │
  │ mentioned        │ 0     │ for all 50 → nobody has biologics "never     │
  │ (absence)        │       │ mentioned"                                   │
  ├──────────────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ discussed but    │       │ 50 prescribed − 37 taken = 13                │
  │ not taken        │ 13    │ prescribed-but-not-taken                     │
  │ (negation)       │       │                                              │
  ├──────────────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ churned /        │       │ the model labels churn = False for all 50 —  │
  │ truncated        │ 0     │ the documented churn under-detection (gold   │
  │ (truncation)     │       │ flags some, e.g. P049, as false negatives)   │
  └──────────────────┴───────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  Two of the three "states" are independent counts pulled from different fields
  (biologic_not_mentioned, biologic_prescribed & ~biologic_taken, churn), so
  they don't partition the 50 — that's why they don't sum to the cohort. The 0s
  are each informative: absence-0 actually flags a possible model over-detection
  (50/50 biologic_prescribed is suspiciously total), and truncation-0 is the
  churn weakness already called out in the eval. I added a titled lead-in above
  the table spelling all this out, so the numbers are interpretable instead of
  mysterious.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

⏺ Now I have the full picture, including the ground truth. Here's the complete
  answer.

  How "absence" is measured

  It's not a keyword scan — it's the model's biologic_not_mentioned boolean, and
  the table just counts how many it set True (= 0). So "absence = 0" is
  entirely the model's judgment, which is exactly what you were right to
  question.

  Your hypothesis — confirmed

  11 transcripts name a biologic (Humira, Remicade, …) without ever using the 
  word "biologic." A naive grep "biologic" would have wrongly called all 11 "not
  mentioned." The model did not make that mistake — it set
  biologic_not_mentioned=False for them correctly. So on the named-only cases,
  the model is smarter than keyword matching.

  The "neither" case — exactly one, P047, and the model gets it wrong

  P047 (Donna) has neither the word nor any named biologic. Reading it confirms
  why: 5 months post-diagnosis, on prednisone → mesalamine → methotrexate, ~30%
  improvement, "doctor says give it more time." No biologic is mentioned,
  recommended, or offered anywhere. Yet the model predicted:

  biologic_prescribed            = True     ← no biologic in the text
  biologic_not_mentioned         = False    ← biologics ARE absent
  reasons_for_biologic_prescribed= DOCTOR_CHOICE   ← fabricated
  methotrexate.before_biologic   = True     ← there is no biologic to be
  "before"

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  This is a textbook hallucination by inference: the model saw a severe,
  escalating Crohn's case on conventional therapy and inferred "a biologic was
  the doctor's choice," even though the transcript never says so. The schema
  defines biologic_prescribed as "was a biologic prescribed/recommended at any 
  point" — for P047 the honest answer is no. So "absence = 0" is wrong by at
  least one: P047 should be the absence case, and biologic_prescribed = 50/50 is
  implausibly total — a sign the over-detection isn't unique to P047.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Why the eval never caught this

  P047 is to_review = 0 — outside the 20-case reviewed set, so it's unannotated
  and the gold eval never scores it.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  This is a real blind spot in the evaluation design: the model's worst funnel
  error sits in the ~30 unreviewed cases, invisible to a metric computed only
  over to_review == 1. It's the same "balanced gold ≠ representative cohort"
  caveat already noted in SCHEMA.md — but here it has teeth: the rate-style
  answers (Q1 "% on a biologic", the absence count) run over all 50, while the
  accuracy check runs over only the 20 humans looked at.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Root cause

  system.txt has explicit guidance for churn, biologic_timing, the reasons
  enums, and referral_pathway — but nothing for the biologic-funnel booleans
  (biologic_prescribed / biologic_taken / biologic_not_mentioned). The model
  fills them from its own clinical priors, which over-assert. (This is precisely
  the open TODO item: "build up the prompt for every field… biologic funnel.")

  Proposed fix (needs a fresh run to take effect)

  Add funnel guidance to system.txt, e.g.:

  ▎ biologic_prescribed / biologic_taken / biologic_not_mentioned: base these 
  ▎ only on what the transcript states. Set biologic_not_mentioned true when no 
  ▎ biologic is referenced — neither the word "biologic" nor any named biologic 
  ▎ (Humira, Remicade, Stelara, Entyvio, infliximab, adalimumab, ustekinumab, 
  ▎ vedolizumab, …). Only set biologic_prescribed true when a biologic was 
  ▎ actually prescribed, recommended, or offered in the text — do not infer it 
  ▎ from disease severity or "next step" reasoning. When biologic_not_mentioned 
  ▎ is true, biologic_prescribed and biologic_taken are false and every 
  ▎ biologic_timing is NO_BIOLOGIC.

  That dovetails with the NO_BIOLOGIC enum we just added (P047's methotrexate
  would correctly become NO_BIOLOGIC instead of before_biologic=True).

  Want me to add that funnel guidance to system.txt now (so it's in place for
  the next extraction), or hold while you consider the wording?

⏺ This is a sharp observation, and the data backs you up. Let me lay out the
  evidence.

  How system.txt currently handles "..."

  The churn rule has effectively two clauses that push toward not churn, and one
  toward churn:

  ▎ - Set true when it ends mid-sentence or mid-word with no resolution (e.g. 
  ▎ cut off at "Chronic...") — a genuine cut-off.
  ▎ - Treat a trailing "..." as a cut-off ONLY if "..." does NOT appear 
  ▎ elsewhere… If "..." recurs as a habit, OR the final sentence reads as a 
  ▎ complete (even if downbeat or emotional) ending, set false.

  So the model is told two different discriminators: (a) does "..." recur, and
  (b) is the final sentence a complete/emotional ending. The genuine-churn
  signal is specifically a mid-word/mid-thought break like "Chronic...".

  The other "..." case we took as churn — P049

  P049 is the one the gold confirms as churn (it's the old incomplete_journey →
  churn merge case, per TO_REVIEW.md):

  P049  …Different but same. Chronic...     (single "...")

  The tell isn't the "..." — it's that it breaks on "Chronic", an adjective with
  no noun. Grammatically incomplete, mid-thought → a genuine cut-off. That's
  real churn.

  The full "..." landscape (from the scan)

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The transcripts split cleanly into two families, and the discriminator is not
  whether "..." recurs — it's whether the text breaks mid-thought or closes on a
  coherent phrase:

  - Mid-thought break (churn): P049 "…Chronic..." — the only true cut-off.
  - Coherent emotional close (NOT churn): P016 "…better than this...", P036
  "…Don't want to know...", P045 "…but it's hard...", P046 "…how people 
  manage...", P047 "…Not worry about mom...", P048 "…I'm only 56...".

  P016 is the precedent: the model flagged it churn, the gold corrected it to 0
  ("stylistic/emotional marker… an ending sentence, not a cut-off").
  P045/46/47/48 are the same shape as P016 — they just happen to use "..." only
  once.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Verdict on P047 — your new doubt is correct

  P047 ends "…They need to focus. Not worry about mom...". That's a coherent, 
  deliberate emotional close, not a mid-thought break. It belongs with P016, not
  P049. Your reasoning nails it: a patient who types "..." is making a 
  keystroke choice — a stylistic trailing-off — which is evidence of engagement,
  not disconnection. A genuine app-cutoff looks like P049 ("Chronic..."), or
  just stops mid-word with no punctuation at all.

  So P047 should be churn = 0, and by the rule's own clause (b) ("final sentence
  reads as a complete… emotional ending → set false") it already should have
  been.

  The flaw this exposes

  The rule's first clause — "treat a trailing "..." as a cut-off ONLY if "..." 
  does not appear elsewhere" — uses recurrence as the discriminator. That's the
  wrong axis: it would flag P045/46/47/48 (single "...", complete phrases) as
  churn while only P049 is real. The recurrence test and clause (b) actively
  contradict each other on exactly these cases.

  The fix is to make "mid-word/mid-thought break vs. coherent close" the primary
  discriminator, and demote the recurrence heuristic:

  ▎ A deliberately typed "..." on a grammatically complete or coherent phrase is
  ▎ an intentional trailing-off, NOT churn — regardless of whether "..." 
  ▎ appears elsewhere. Set churn = true only when the text breaks off mid-word 
  ▎ or mid-thought with no resolution (e.g. ends on "Chronic..."), which signals
  ▎ the interview was actually cut off.

  What I'd do (with your confirmation, since it cascades)

  1. Flip P047 gold churn → 0 in _v5.ods (it's an intentional ending, like
  P016).
  2. Sharpen the system.txt churn rule as above.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  Cascade to be aware of: churn is not in gold_eval (that scores
  biologic_taken), so the n=20 / 9-1-0-10 metrics are unaffected. But flipping
  P047 changes churn_false_negatives and the churn three-state table prose. And
  note: the model already predicts churn=False for P047, so making gold P047=0
  turns a would-be disagreement into agreement — it actually improves the
  apparent churn accuracy, which is the honest outcome here.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Want me to (a) flip P047 to churn=0 and (b) sharpen the system.txt rule — and
  should I apply the same "coherent close = not churn" logic as the standard
  going forward (which would also reaffirm P045/46/48 as not-churn if they're
  ever reviewed)?

⏺ Good question — they count different things, and the gap is one specific case.
  Let me pinpoint it:
  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Why the two numbers differ

  They measure different things, and the gap is one specific case — P032:

  - Reviewed set = 21 — cases a human marked to_review == 1 (i.e. looked at / in
  annotation scope).
  - Eval n = 20 — the subset of those that also have a non-null gold 
  biologic_taken to score the model against.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The gap comes from one line in gold_eval (src/post_extraction_eda.py:53):
  gold = gold.loc[gold["to_review"] == 1, ["patient_id", field]].dropna()
  The .dropna() drops any reviewed row whose biologic_taken cell is blank. P032
  is to_review == 1 (reviewed) but its biologic_taken was never filled in → it's
  excluded from the biologic-taken metric. So 21 reviewed − 1 blank (P032) = 20
  scored.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  The before/after, precisely:

  ┌────────────────────────────┬───────────────────┬───────────────────────┐
  │                            │     Reviewed      │   Eval n (non-null    │
  │                            │  (to_review==1)   │    biologic_taken)    │
  ├────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ Before P047                │ 20                │ 19 (P032 blank)       │
  ├────────────────────────────┼───────────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ After P047 (to_review=1,   │ 21                │ 20 (P032 still blank) │
  │ biologic_taken=0)          │                   │                       │
  └────────────────────────────┴───────────────────┴───────────────────────┘

  P047 has a non-null biologic_taken (you set it to 0), so it lands in both
  counts — pushing reviewed 20→21 and eval 19→20. P032 remains the one
  reviewed-but-not-scored case.

  ---
  Now let me apply the resulting number cascade. First the gate-critical test
  update (it currently asserts the old n=19, 9/1/0/9):

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

❯ you could programmatically set the latest .ods file in README.qmd from        
  config.json

  kills this whole class of version-bump drift (exactly the churn we just did
  manually). Let me do that. First the gate-critical test update, and read the
  README setup chunk to wire in the dynamic filename:

      311  
      312  The model flagged `churn = true` for only **`{python} int((df[
           "churn"] == True).sum())`/`{python} N`** patient(s). Manual re
           view of the flagged/edge cases (`docs/TO_REVIEW.md`) found chu
           rn detection unreliable in both directions — both an over-flag
            and a missed truncation — because truncation is a lexical/str
           uctural property of the transcript's *end* that the narrative 
           under-determines. **Q1 and Q3 are the most trustworthy** answe
           rs (they depend on facts stated early); **Q4 is the least** (i
           t depends on the full pathway surviving, and on the under-emit
           ted GP node).
      313  
      314 -> **Why `churn` reads low — under-detection, not absence.** Th
          -e gold set flags genuinely truncated transcripts the model mis
          -ses (gold `churn = 1` but the model returns `False`; this run:
          - **`{python} churn_fn_str`**) — a **false negative**. A low co
          -unt is therefore *not* "no truncated transcripts": the model *
          -*can't reliably detect truncation** — a lexical/structural end
          --of-text signal the narrative under-determines, compounded by 
          -the prompt's *"prefer `false` when ambiguous"* bias. This is *
          -*independent of `temperature`** (it persists at `0`); pinning 
          -`temperature = 0` (see *Extraction Pipeline → Determinism*) ma
          -kes the count reproducible run-to-run, but the real fix is a d
          -eterministic tail-of-text rule (`docs/TODO.md`). The temperatu
          -re change is about *reproducibility*, not detection: earlier *
          -*exploratory/testing** runs used Gemini's default **`temperatu
          -re = 1.0`** (sampled — long-tail outputs drifted run-to-run), 
          -whereas this **final pipeline pins `temperature = 0`** (greedy
          -, deterministic). Same under-detection either way; only the ru
          -n-to-run stability differs.

      27  **Answers.**
      28  
      29  - **Enums vs. free text vs. objects.** Closed vocabularies the 
          analysis groups on are **enums** — `TreatmentOutcome`, `ReasonP
          rescribed`, `ReasonNotTaken`, `ComorbidCondition`, `PathwayStep
          ` — so `value_counts`/joins get stable categories and membershi
          p is checked at the model boundary (`enforce_validation`). Open
          , high-cardinality, not-yet-canonical fields stay **free text**
          : `biologic_type`, `TreatmentRecord.name`/`treatment_class` (a 
          branded-drug + treatment-class registry is future work — `docs/
          TODO.md`). Repeated, multi-attribute things are **structured ob
          jects/lists**: `treatment_records` (a list of `TreatmentRecord`
          , each carrying `biologic_timing`, the field that answers Q3), 
          `demographics`, and `referral_pathway` (`list[PathwayStep]`). T
          he visible cost of the free-text choice shows up in the **Q3 ta
          ble** — `conventional_therapy`/`conventional` and case variants
           are one class left un-normalised.
      30 -- **Three states, not one null.** "Not mentioned" (absence), "e
         -xplicitly denied" (negation) and "cut off" (truncation) are kep
         -t as *separate* signals rather than collapsed into a null: abse
         -nce → `biologic_not_mentioned` + null/empty fields + `NOT_MENTI
         -ONED`; negation → the `biologic_prescribed` vs. `biologic_taken
         -` pair + a specific `ReasonNotTaken` (`EXPLICIT_DENIAL`/`INSURA
         -NCE_PROBLEMS`/…); truncation → `churn` + `JOURNEY_CUT_OFF`. A `
         -NOT_APPLICABLE` member marks "genuinely doesn't apply" so it is
         -n't confused with a blank "not yet annotated" cell. This is the
         - split the churn table under Q4 operationalises.               
      30 +- **Three states, not one null.** "Not mentioned" (absence), "e
         +xplicitly denied" (negation) and "cut off" (truncation) are kep
         +t as *separate* signals rather than collapsed into a null: abse
         +nce → `biologic_not_mentioned` + null/empty fields + `NOT_MENTI
         +ONED`; negation → the `biologic_prescribed` vs. `biologic_taken
         +` pair + a specific `ReasonNotTaken` (`EXPLICIT_DENIAL`/`INSURA
         +NCE_PROBLEMS`/…); truncation → `churn` (a cut-off journey is th
         +e same truncation signal `churn` already carries, so there is n
         +o separate `JOURNEY_CUT_OFF` reason — it was folded into `churn
         +`, like `incomplete_journey` before it). A `NOT_APPLICABLE` mem
         +ber marks "genuinely doesn't apply" so it isn't confused with a
         + blank "not yet annotated" cell. This is the split the churn ta
         +ble under Q4 operationalises.

          nce_notes`** — a free-text rationale per patient (all 50 predic

          loss of response; currently on Stelara; injection anxiety"*). W
          hat's **deferred is the manual side**: the gold annotation keep
          s `evidence_notes` as an unfilled column (`docs/SCHEMA.md`), an
          d structured **per-field** evidence — a snippet/turn-ref *per f
          ield* rather than one blob — is stretch task 2 (it costs tokens
           + prompt complexity). The broader audit trail is the **logged 
          raw model output** (`logs/extract.log` keeps the system prompt,
           schema, input and pre-validation response per call) plus the *

⏺ Both answered, with the data behind them.

  "per-field evidence is deferred" — what it means

  evidence_notes currently holds one free-text rationale for the whole patient
  (a single blob, e.g. "cycled through Remicade, Humira due to loss of response;
  currently on Stelara; injection anxiety"). Per-field evidence is the
  deferred, more granular version: a supporting snippet / turn-reference for 
  each field (so a reviewer can trace biologic_taken=true to the exact
  transcript span that justifies it), rather than one patient-level summary.
  It's deferred because it costs tokens + prompt complexity.

  Is it in the TODO? No — it's documented only in README.qmd (the Schema Design
  question at line 24, the answer at line 31 calling it "stretch task 2", and
  the related "per-field confidence scoring" stretch at line 370). It is not in
  docs/TODO.md. I can add a one-line TODO entry so the deferral is tracked where
  the other deferred work lives — say the word.

  ACCESS — the full picture

  What it means: honestly, it's underspecified — there's no original definition
  anywhere, so the comment I added ("access/coverage drove the choice") is my
  interpretation, not a recovered spec. And it's never used: the model picks
  DOCTOR_CHOICE for all 50, and the gold never uses ACCESS.

  Is it in system.txt? Yes — line 17 lists it: "...(DOCTOR_CHOICE, 
  PATIENT_FEARS, COST, ACCESS, OTHER)..." — but without a definition, so the
  model has no guidance to apply it consistently (same gap that caused the
  biologic-funnel problem).

  Does the prompt align with schema.py? Yes — and structurally guaranteed:

  ┌────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┬────────┬─────────┐
  │                        │                            │ schema │         │
  │         Field          │      system.txt lists      │  .py   │ Aligned │
  │                        │                            │  enum  │         │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼────────┼─────────┤
  │                        │ all 6 (DOCTOR_CHOICE,      │        │         │
  │ reasons_for_biologic_p │ PATIENT_FEARS, COST,       │ same 6 │ ✓ exact │
  │ rescribed              │ ACCESS, OTHER,             │        │         │
  │                        │ NOT_APPLICABLE)            │        │         │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼────────┼─────────┤
  │ treatment_outcome      │ all 6                      │ same 6 │ ✓ exact │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼────────┼─────────┤
  │ biologic_timing        │ all 4                      │ same 4 │ ✓ exact │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼────────┼─────────┤
  │ reasons_for_biologic_n │ examples + UNKNOWN/NOT_APP │ 10     │ ✓ (exam │
  │ ot_taken               │ LICABLE/NOT_MENTIONED      │ values │ ples)   │
  ├────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼────────┼─────────┤
  │ comorbid_conditions    │ examples + OTHER           │ 15     │ ✓ (exam │
  │                        │                            │ values │ ples)   │
  └────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┴────────┴─────────┘

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  Alignment is enforced, not just described: the call sends
  PatientLabels.model_json_schema() as the response_schema with
  enforce_validation: True, so Gemini literally cannot return a value outside
  each enum — regardless of what the prompt lists. The prompt's value-lists are
  guidance to improve accuracy, not the enforcement mechanism. That's why a
  listed-but-undefined value like ACCESS is "aligned" yet useless: it's valid
  per the schema, but its meaning isn't pinned, so the model never reaches for
  it.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  So ACCESS is in the same boat as JOURNEY_CUT_OFF was — present, unused,
  ambiguous. That's a decision for you:

⏺ Now I have the complete picture. Here's the full investigation.

  1. "Only the expected q3 stale-data failure" — what it means

  The gate's one failing test is test_q3_before_biologic_nonempty. It reads
  data/out/ and runs q3_before_biologic, which now filters biologic_timing == 
  "BEFORE". But data/out/ is the old run — its treatments carry the old
  before_biologic field, not biologic_timing — so the filter matches nothing →
  empty → assert sum > 0 fails. It's "expected" (code is ahead of data, by your
  earlier agreement) and resolves on the next extraction run. Confirmed by the
  audit: TreatmentRecord.biologic_timing usage = {} (empty) in current data.

  2. Why DOCTOR_CHOICE is 50/50 — investigated

  Three compounding causes:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  1. biologic_prescribed was over-set to True for all 50 (the same hallucination
  we found via P047). So reasons_for_biologic_prescribed gets populated for 
  every patient — there are no NOT_APPLICABLE cases (which is why NOT_APPLICABLE
  shows 0 uses).
  2. The old prompt gave zero guidance for this field, so the model falls back
  to the most generically-true option.
  3. DOCTOR_CHOICE is that generic catch-all — "the doctor recommended it" is
  trivially true for almost any biologic case, so with nothing to differentiate,
  it wins every time. The other ReasonPrescribed values are never reached.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  3. ACCESS — the evidence resolves it

  ACCESS is real (from the business spec) but misfiled. The spec lists it as a
  reason a patient is not on a biologic — an access/coverage barrier. Yet
  schema.py put ACCESS in ReasonPrescribed ("why a biologic was chosen"), where
  a barrier makes no sense — which is exactly why it's never used. And it
  overlaps with INSURANCE_PROBLEMS (denial/auth/tier) already in ReasonNotTaken.

  So the spec's Q2 reasons — doctor choice / patient fears / cost / access — map
  to not-taken, but the schema scattered them: DOCTOR_CHOICE + ACCESS landed in
  ReasonPrescribed, while PATIENT_FEARS/COST/INSURANCE_PROBLEMS are in
  ReasonNotTaken. That's a genuine misalignment with the spec.

  4. Full enum audit (your "all used + vice versa + SCHEMA.md aligns")

  Never-used enum values (in current/old data):
  - ReasonPrescribed: only DOCTOR_CHOICE used; ACCESS, COST, PATIENT_FEARS, 
  OTHER, NOT_APPLICABLE never (see §2/§3).
  - ReasonNotTaken: EXPLICIT_DENIAL, NOT_MENTIONED, OTHER, UNKNOWN never used.
  - TreatmentOutcome: AMBIGUOUS, SUCCESS never at patient level (SUCCESS unused
  — Crohn's rarely "cured"); AMBIGUOUS never anywhere.
  - PathwayStep: biologic_not_taken, remission, unresolved, other never emitted.
  - BiologicTiming: none yet (stale data).
  - ComorbidCondition: all 15 used — but OTHER=30, a strong signal the enum is
  missing tokens (bipolar, COPD, PSC… already flagged in SCHEMA.md).

  Off-vocab in the gold (_v6.ods) — values that aren't valid enum members:
  - reasons_for_biologic_prescribed: NOT PRESCRIBED, NOT MENTIONED
  - reasons_for_biologic_not_taken: BIOLOGIC WAS TAKEN, NOT PRESCRIBED, NOT 
  MENTIONED
  - These are your hand-entered shorthand; the schema-correct value is
  NOT_APPLICABLE / NOT_MENTIONED. They don't break the gate (only biologic_taken
  is scored), but they don't align with the vocab.

  data/out predictions: all in-vocab (schema-validated), so no off-vocab there.
  SCHEMA.md: the controlled-vocab list and the Pydantic sketch now match
  schema.py (JOURNEY_CUT_OFF gone from both; biologic_timing/BiologicTiming in
  both).

  5. README — enforce_validation point added

  Added to the Schema Design enums answer: "Gemini literally cannot return a 
  value outside each enum, regardless of what the prompt lists."

  ---
  These findings open three real schema decisions — I'll hold and let you direct
  rather than re-asking a rigid menu:

  1. ACCESS: remove from ReasonPrescribed (misfiled + redundant with
  INSURANCE_PROBLEMS), or realign the reason enums to the spec's Q2 list (doctor
  choice / patient fears / cost / access)?
  2. Other never-used values (e.g. ReasonPrescribed alternatives, AMBIGUOUS,
  PathwayStep remission/unresolved): prune like JOURNEY_CUT_OFF, or keep as
  valid-but-unhit (some, like remission/SUCCESS, are legitimately rare, not
  wrong)?
  3. Gold off-vocab (NOT PRESCRIBED etc.): normalise to NOT_APPLICABLE in a _v6
  rebuild?

  How would you like to proceed on these?
