⏺ The subagent reviewed all 50 transcripts against the spec — strong,
  evidence-backed conclusions. Let me answer your immediate question, then give
  the evaluation you asked for.

  What "normalise gold off-vocab to NOT_APPLICABLE in a _v6 rebuild" means

  You hand-typed shorthand into the gold's reason columns — NOT PRESCRIBED, NOT 
  MENTIONED, BIOLOGIC WAS TAKEN — which aren't valid enum members. The schema's
  canonical "doesn't apply" value is NOT_APPLICABLE (and NOT_MENTIONED for
  absence). "Normalise in a rebuild" = run a small sandbox script (like
  build_ground_truth_v6.py) over the .ods that replaces those strings with the
  canonical enum values, so the gold uses the same controlled vocabulary as
  schema.py. It doesn't affect the gate today (only biologic_taken is scored),
  but it keeps the gold consistent. Best bundled with the enum redesign below —
  no point normalising to NOT_APPLICABLE now if we then change the enums.

  Evaluation: enums vs the TASK_INSTRUCTIONS spec (evidence from all 50 
  transcripts)

  The spec's Q2 list — doctor choice, patient fears, cost, access, something 
  else — describes reasons a patient is not on a biologic. Here's how each holds
  up against the data:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  ACCESS adds nothing — drop it. Zero of the 50 patients have a true
  access/availability barrier (drug unobtainable). Every "can't get it" reduces
  to affordability (COST — P004 "$5,000 per infusion"), payer-gating
  (INSURANCE_PROBLEMS — step-therapy "fail more drugs first" is the single
  biggest barrier: P002/P016/P024/P032/P045), or medical (CONTRAINDICATION —
  P050 COPD). The spec says "access," but the evidence says the barrier is
  payer-side gating, not availability — so we depart from the spec's wording 
  with evidence and keep INSURANCE_PROBLEMS instead of ACCESS.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Reasons co-occur → the field should be multi-select. At least 3 (up to 6)
  not-taken patients have genuinely multiple simultaneous reasons: P002 (cost +
  insurance), P016 (step-therapy + fears), P018 (fears + cost). A single scalar
  forces a lossy choice. Recommend: reasons_for_biologic_not_taken: 
  list[ReasonNotTaken] + an optional primary_reason — which also directly
  satisfies the spec's "primary reason" phrasing while preserving co-occurrence.

  DOCTOR_CHOICE 50/50 is partly genuine, partly artifact. Initiation is
  doctor-driven in ~all 50 (patients react; only P017/P024 actively advocate,
  never alone). So DOCTOR_CHOICE dominating is real. But it's filled for all 50
  only because biologic_prescribed was over-detected to True everywhere (the
  P047 bug). And ReasonPrescribed's other members (COST/ACCESS/PATIENT_FEARS)
  are misfiled — they're not-taken reasons, which is why they're never used.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  ReasonPrescribed is near-degenerate: it collapses to DOCTOR_CHOICE, and the
  spec never actually asks "why prescribed" (Q2 is "why not on a biologic"). A
  PATIENT_REQUEST counterpart would match only ~2 patients and never as a sole
  driver; patient resistance (~7) is already captured by PATIENT_FEARS. So the
  high-value field is reasons_for_biologic_not_taken; ReasonPrescribed could be
  trimmed to {DOCTOR_CHOICE, PATIENT_REQUEST, OTHER, NOT_APPLICABLE} or dropped.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Proposed concrete changes (for your go-ahead)

  1. Drop ACCESS from ReasonPrescribed (0 evidence; covered by COST +
  INSURANCE_PROBLEMS) — like we did JOURNEY_CUT_OFF.
  2. Make reasons_for_biologic_not_taken a list[ReasonNotTaken] + add
  primary_reason_for_biologic_not_taken (single) to answer the spec's "primary
  reason."
  3. Trim ReasonPrescribed to {DOCTOR_CHOICE, PATIENT_REQUEST, OTHER,
  NOT_APPLICABLE} (or drop the field) — the misfiled COST/PATIENT_FEARS belong
  only in the not-taken enum.
  4. Then normalise the gold off-vocab + rebuild _v6, update
  SCHEMA.md/system.txt/README, and the changes take effect on the next
  extraction run.

  These are real schema changes (and #2 touches schema.py, the prompt, the EDA,
  and the gold), so I'll hold for your direction. Which of 1–4 do you want, and
  should ReasonPrescribed be trimmed or dropped entirely?

⏺ Update(README.qmd)
  ⎿  Added 1 line, removed 1 line
      27  **Answers.**
      28  
      29  - **Enums vs. free text vs. objects.** Closed vocabularies the 
          analysis groups on are **enums** — `TreatmentOutcome`, `ReasonP
          rescribed`, `ReasonNotTaken`, `ComorbidCondition`, `PathwayStep
          ` — so `value_counts`/joins get stable categories and membershi
          p is checked at the model boundary (`enforce_validation`) — **G
          emini literally cannot return a value outside each enum, regard
          less of what the prompt lists** (the schema is sent as `respons
          e_schema` and the reply is validated, raising `JSONSchemaValida
          tionError` on a mismatch). Open, high-cardinality, not-yet-cano
          nical fields stay **free text**: `biologic_type`, `TreatmentRec
          ord.name`/`treatment_class` (a branded-drug + treatment-class r
          egistry is future work — `docs/TODO.md`). Repeated, multi-attri
          bute things are **structured objects/lists**: `treatment_record
          s` (a list of `TreatmentRecord`, each carrying `biologic_timing
          `, the field that answers Q3), `demographics`, and `referral_pa
          thway` (`list[PathwayStep]`). The visible cost of the free-text
           choice shows up in the **Q3 table** — `conventional_therapy`/`
          conventional` and case variants are one class left un-normalise
          d.
      30 -- **Three states, not one null.** "Not mentioned" (absence), "e
         -xplicitly denied" (negation) and "cut off" (truncation) are kep
         -t as *separate* signals rather than collapsed into a null: abse
         -nce → `biologic_not_mentioned` + null/empty fields + `NOT_MENTI
         -ONED`; negation → the `biologic_prescribed` vs. `biologic_taken
         -` pair + a specific `ReasonNotTaken` (`EXPLICIT_DENIAL`/`INSURA
         -NCE_PROBLEMS`/…); truncation → `churn` (a cut-off journey is th
         -e same truncation signal `churn` already carries, so there is n
         -o separate `JOURNEY_CUT_OFF` reason — it was folded into `churn
         -`, like `incomplete_journey` before it). A `NOT_APPLICABLE` mem
         -ber marks "genuinely doesn't apply" so it isn't confused with a
         - blank "not yet annotated" cell. This is the split the churn ta
         -ble under Q4 operationalises.                                  
      30 +- **Three states, not one null.** "Not mentioned" (absence), "e
         +xplicitly denied" (negation) and "cut off" (truncation) are kep
         +t as *separate* signals rather than collapsed into a null: abse
         +nce → `biologic_not_mentioned` + null/empty fields + `NOT_MENTI
         +ONED`; negation → the `biologic_prescribed` vs. `biologic_taken
         +` pair + one or more `ReasonNotTaken` (`EXPLICIT_DENIAL`/`INSUR
         +ANCE_PROBLEMS`/…; multi-select); truncation → `churn` (a cut-of
         +f journey is the same truncation signal `churn` already carries
         +, so there is no separate `JOURNEY_CUT_OFF` reason — it was fol
         +ded into `churn`, like `incomplete_journey` before it). A `NOT_
         +APPLICABLE` member marks "genuinely doesn't apply" so it isn't 
         +confused with a blank "not yet annotated" cell. This is the spl
         +it the churn table under Q4 operationalises.                   
      31  - **Evidence / auditability.** The model **does populate `evide
          nce_notes`** — a free-text rationale per patient (all 50 predic
          tions carry one, e.g. *"cycled through Remicade, Humira due to 
          loss of response; currently on Stelara; injection anxiety"*). W
          hat's **deferred is the manual side**: the gold annotation keep
          s `evidence_notes` as an unfilled column (`docs/SCHEMA.md`), an
          d structured **per-field** evidence — a snippet/turn-ref *per f
          ield* rather than one blob — is stretch task 2 (it costs tokens
           + prompt complexity). The broader audit trail is the **logged 
          raw model output** (`logs/extract.log` keeps the system prompt,
           schema, input and pre-validation response per call) plus the *
          *gold-set comparison** (`docs/TO_REVIEW.md`).
      32  - **Socio-demographics.** Deliberately minimal — `Demographics{
          gender, age}`, self-reported, grouped so a later "do pathways/o
          utcomes differ by demographic" cut is possible; everything else
           is noise for these four questions.
      33  
⏺ All four tasks complete. Here's the full change set, ready for your commit.

  Reason enums realigned to the spec (evidence from all 50 transcripts)

  1. Dropped ACCESS — zero transcript support (every "can't get it" is COST or
  INSURANCE_PROBLEMS; "access" in the data is only bathroom access). It was
  misfiled in ReasonPrescribed anyway.

  2. Trimmed ReasonPrescribed → {DOCTOR_CHOICE, PATIENT_REQUEST, NOT_APPLICABLE,
  OTHER}. Removed the misfiled COST/PATIENT_FEARS/ACCESS (those are not-taken
  reasons); added PATIENT_REQUEST for the rare patient-advocacy cases
  (P017/P024).

  3. reasons_for_biologic_not_taken is now a list (list[ReasonNotTaken], primary
  first) — the evidence showed patients genuinely co-occur reasons (P002
  cost+insurance, P016 step-therapy+fears, P018 fears+cost).

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  This also explains the DOCTOR_CHOICE 50/50 you flagged: initiation is
  near-universally doctor-driven (genuine), but it was inflated because
  biologic_prescribed was over-detected to True for all 50, populating the
  reason for everyone. The redesign attacks both ends — PATIENT_REQUEST captures
  the real exceptions, and the not-taken list stops forcing one lossy reason
  per patient.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  - schema.py (enum trim + list field), system.txt (both reason fields,
  PATIENT_REQUEST defined, multi-reason instruction), post_extraction_eda.py
  (q2_reasons now counts reason-mentions), the q2 test (mentions, ≥), README.qmd
  (Q2 chart + "three states" note), SCHEMA.md (column type, vocab, sketch, v0.7
  changelog).
  - Gold rebuilt via build_ground_truth_v6.py: off-vocab NOT PRESCRIBED / NOT 
  MENTIONED / BIOLOGIC WAS TAKEN → NOT_APPLICABLE / NOT_MENTIONED. Gold reason
  columns now 100% in-vocab.

⏺ Update(README.qmd)
  ⎿  Added 1 line
      28  
      29  - **Enums vs. free text vs. objects.** Closed vocabularies the 
          analysis groups on are **enums** — `TreatmentOutcome`, `ReasonP
          rescribed`, `ReasonNotTaken`, `ComorbidCondition`, `PathwayStep
          ` — so `value_counts`/joins get stable categories and membershi
          p is checked at the model boundary (`enforce_validation`) — **G
          emini literally cannot return a value outside each enum, regard
          less of what the prompt lists** (the schema is sent as `respons
          e_schema` and the reply is validated, raising `JSONSchemaValida
          tionError` on a mismatch). Open, high-cardinality, not-yet-cano
          nical fields stay **free text**: `biologic_type`, `TreatmentRec
          ord.name`/`treatment_class` (a branded-drug + treatment-class r
          egistry is future work — `docs/TODO.md`). Repeated, multi-attri
          bute things are **structured objects/lists**: `treatment_record
          s` (a list of `TreatmentRecord`, each carrying `biologic_timing
          `, the field that answers Q3), `demographics`, and `referral_pa
          thway` (`list[PathwayStep]`). The visible cost of the free-text
           choice shows up in the **Q3 table** — `conventional_therapy`/`
          conventional` and case variants are one class left un-normalise
          d.
      30  - **Three states, not one null.** "Not mentioned" (absence), "e
          xplicitly denied" (negation) and "cut off" (truncation) are kep
          t as *separate* signals rather than collapsed into a null: abse
          nce → `biologic_not_mentioned` + null/empty fields + `NOT_MENTI
          ONED`; negation → the `biologic_prescribed` vs. `biologic_taken
          ` pair + one or more `ReasonNotTaken` (`EXPLICIT_DENIAL`/`INSUR
          ANCE_PROBLEMS`/…; multi-select); truncation → `churn` (a cut-of
          f journey is the same truncation signal `churn` already carries
          , so there is no separate `JOURNEY_CUT_OFF` reason — it was fol
          ded into `churn`, like `incomplete_journey` before it). A `NOT_
          APPLICABLE` member marks "genuinely doesn't apply" so it isn't 
          confused with a blank "not yet annotated" cell. This is the spl
          it the churn table under Q4 operationalises.
      31 +- **Reason enums — aligned to the spec, departing only where th
         +e evidence demanded.** The spec's Q2 reasons (*doctor choice / 
         +patient fears / cost / access*) shaped these enums, but reviewi
         +ng all 50 transcripts drove two deliberate calls (evidenced in 
         +[`docs/SCHEMA.md`](docs/SCHEMA.md) v0.7): **(1)** we keep `INSU
         +RANCE_PROBLEMS` rather than the spec's bare *"access"* — every 
         +real barrier in the data is payer-gating (step-therapy / prior-
         +auth / tier), not drug *availability*, and `ACCESS` had **zero*
         +* transcript support (covered by `COST` + `INSURANCE_PROBLEMS`)
         +; **(2)** `reasons_for_biologic_not_taken` is **multi-select** 
         +(a list), because patients routinely have several reasons at on
         +ce (e.g. cost + fear). Relatedly, `ReasonPrescribed` was trimme
         +d to the initiation signal (`DOCTOR_CHOICE` / `PATIENT_REQUEST`
         +), since cost / fears / access are reasons a biologic is *not t
         +aken*, not reasons it was prescribed.                          
      32  - **Evidence / auditability.** The model **does populate `evide
          nce_notes`** — a free-text rationale per patient (all 50 predic
          tions carry one, e.g. *"cycled through Remicade, Humira due to 
          loss of response; currently on Stelara; injection anxiety"*). W
          hat's **deferred is the manual side**: the gold annotation keep
          s `evidence_notes` as an unfilled column (`docs/SCHEMA.md`), an
          d structured **per-field** evidence — a snippet/turn-ref *per f
          ield* rather than one blob — is stretch task 2 (it costs tokens
           + prompt complexity). The broader audit trail is the **logged 
          raw model output** (`logs/extract.log` keeps the system prompt,
           schema, input and pre-validation response per call) plus the *
          *gold-set comparison** (`docs/TO_REVIEW.md`).
      33  - **Socio-demographics.** Deliberately minimal — `Demographics{
          gender, age}`, self-reported, grouped so a later "do pathways/o
          utcomes differ by demographic" cut is possible; everything else
           is noise for these four questions.

⏺ Update(README.qmd)
  ⎿  Added 1 line, removed 1 line
      43  **Answers.**
      44  
      45  - **Single-shot, batched — not multi-stage.** One system prompt
           extracts *all* `PatientLabels` fields in a single structured c
          all per patient; transcripts are then **batched** (10 per reque
          st) only to fit Gemini's free-tier ~20-requests/day cap — batch
          ing ≠ staging, it's still one pass over every field, just sever
          al patients per call. Single-shot minimises calls (decisive und
          er the rate cap), keeps the code simple, and lets the model set
           inter-dependent fields (`biologic_prescribed`/`biologic_taken`
          , `churn`, `referral_pathway`) with the whole story in view. Th
          e cost: every field competes for attention in one prompt, so th
          e hard fields suffer — the EDA shows `churn` unreliable and the
           GP node under-emitted. A multi-stage split (per-section prompt
          s run concurrently, with inspectable intermediates) is the #1 i
          tem in *Next Steps* below; it trades more calls + orchestration
           for better per-field accuracy.
      46 -- **Uncertainty: absence vs. negation vs. truncation.** The pro
         -mpt separates them by construction, onto the three schema mecha
         -nisms above. A global rule — *"Leave a field null or empty when
         - the transcript does not support a confident value"* — covers *
         -*absence**. **Negation** rides on the prescribed-vs-taken boole
         -ans + the `ReasonNotTaken` enum. **Truncation** gets the most e
         -xplicit instruction: judge `churn` from how the transcript *end
         -s*, treat a trailing "…" as a cut-off *only* if "…" isn't a rec
         -urring stylistic device, prefer `false` when ambiguous, and — c
         -ritically — *"keep this truncation signal distinct from a topic
         - simply being absent … (that is 'not mentioned', not churn)."* 
         -Honest limit: truncation is a lexical/structural property of th
         -e transcript's end that the narrative under-determines, so the 
         -instruction alone isn't enough — the gold audit found it unreli
         -able in both directions (over-flagging and missed truncation), 
         -and a deterministic tail-of-text rule would do better (`docs/TO
         -_REVIEW.md`).                                                  
      46 +- **Uncertainty: absence vs. negation vs. truncation.** The pro
         +mpt separates them by construction, onto the three schema mecha
         +nisms above. A global rule — *"Leave a field null or empty when
         + the transcript does not support a confident value"* — covers *
         +*absence**. **Negation** rides on the prescribed-vs-taken boole
         +ans + the `ReasonNotTaken` enum. **Truncation** gets the most e
         +xplicit instruction: judge `churn` from how the transcript *end
         +s* — churn **only** when it breaks off mid-word / mid-thought (
         +e.g. ends on "Chronic…"), while a deliberately-typed "…" on a c
         +oherent phrase is an intentional sign-off, **not** churn (no re
         +currence heuristic) — and — critically — *"keep this truncation
         + signal distinct from a topic simply being absent … (that is 'n
         +ot mentioned', not churn)."* Honest limit: truncation is a lexi
         +cal/structural property of the transcript's end that the narrat
         +ive under-determines, so the instruction alone isn't enough — t
         +he gold audit found it unreliable in both directions (over-flag
         +ging and missed truncation), and a deterministic tail-of-text r
         +ule would do better (`docs/TO_REVIEW.md`).                     
      47  - **Determinism & reproducibility.** Three levers, in decreasin
          g order of leverage. **(1) Structured-output mode** — `response
          _format={"type": "json_object", "response_schema": PatientLabel
          s.model_json_schema(), "enforce_validation": true}`: a Pydantic
          -derived schema constrains the *shape* and enum membership, and
           LiteLLM validates the reply (raising `JSONSchemaValidationErro
          r` on a mismatch), so format/parse variance is removed and down
          stream pandas never meets an off-vocabulary value. **(2) Persis
          ted predictions** — each chunk writes one validated JSON per pa
          tient to `data/out/`, and the whole analysis (this README) read
          s *only* those frozen artefacts with no API call, so every numb
          er and plot is **exactly reproducible run-to-run** regardless o
          f LLM nondeterminism; this is the real guarantee. **(3) Decodin
          g params** — `temperature` is **pinned to `0`** (greedy) in `co
          nfig.json` and imported by `extract.py` — extraction here is a 
          **classification** task, so we want *same transcript → same lab
          els*, not sampled variety. This build is the live test case for
           *why*: a fresh re-run at the provider default (Gemini's defaul
          t `temperature` is `1.0`) shifted long-tail outputs (the GP-nod
          e count; which biologics got mislabelled on `biologic_timing`) 
          while the headline answers held — exactly the run-to-run drift 
          `0` removes. Temperature is **logged per run** (with the model)
           so runs at different parameter levels stay comparable. A seed 
          would add belt-and-braces, but with a reasoning-capable model `
          0` *minimises* rather than *guarantees* bit-identical output; *
          *(2)** is the hard reproducibility guarantee for the analysis. 
          No explicit prompt **caching** yet — the static system prompt +
           schema are identical across calls, so implicit context caching
           may apply, and cached-token counts are logged (`docs/TELEMETRY
          .md`).

⏺ dg dev takes -p/--port (confirmed). On your config.json question — let me give
  you the honest tradeoff, then implement.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  There's a subtlety: dg dev -p <port> is a CLI flag the Dagster tool reads, not
  something our Python imports — so config.json can't auto-inject it the way it
  drives MODEL/paths (which config.py actually reads at runtime). Putting it in
  config.json makes it the documented single source (one place to see/change
  it, and config.py can expose it for any future wrapper), but the launch
  command still has to pass -p, so the command mirrors the config value rather
  than being driven by it. That's the standard pattern for a CLI-flag setting,
  and it's still better than the port being hardcoded in ~5 docs — so yes, worth
  centralising.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  I'll use 3050 (avoids your 3000/7234). Reading dagster.yaml for the telemetry
  edit, then making the changes:

  Why the bare command still used 3000

  You ran uv run dg dev -m pipeline -d src without -p 3050, so Dagster fell back
  to its built-in default (3000). This is the caveat from earlier, now seen
  live:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  config.json drives values our Python imports (config.MODEL, paths, CHUNK_SIZE
  — read at runtime). dg dev is an external CLI tool; it reads the port from its
  own -p flag and has no idea config.json exists. So config.json → dagster_port
  is the documented single source and config.py exposes DAGSTER_PORT for any
  wrapper, but it can't auto-inject into dg dev. The command must pass -p.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  So either use the documented command:
  uv run dg dev -m pipeline -d src -p 3050
  …or, to make it zero-typing and genuinely config-driven, add a thin wrapper
  that reads the port from config.json. That's the only way to close the gap.
  I'd suggest a tiny Makefile (the project has none yet):

  dagster:   ## dg dev on config.json's dagster_port
        uv run dg dev -m pipeline -d src -p $$(uv run python -c "import
  json;print(json.load(open('config.json'))['dagster_port'])")
  docsite:   ## mkdocs serve on config.json's docsite_port
        uv run mkdocs serve -a localhost:$$(uv run python -c "import
  json;print(json.load(open('config.json'))['docsite_port'])")
  Then make dagster / make docsite always honour config.json with no flag to
  remember — config.json becomes the actual driver, not just documentation.

  Audit result (your "all other hardcoded values" check)

  - Scripts (src/*.py): clean. Every data/in / data/out / gemini / .ods hit is
  in a docstring or comment (descriptive); the runtime code reads config for all
  of it. No script hardcodes a config value.
  - model, paths, chunk_size, temperature, max_tokens, retries: already in
  config.json and read by the code; doc mentions are descriptive prose (fine).
  - Only two genuinely-hardcoded CLI values existed: the Dagster port (now
  dagster_port) and the MkDocs port (now docsite_port) — both centralised in
  config.json + exposed in config.py, with all 3 mkdocs doc spots and all
  Dagster doc spots updated.

  Want me to add that Makefile so config.json actually drives the launch ports
  (and make dagster / make docsite just work)? Or keep it as the documented -p
  commands?

  How to finish without re-spending on P001–P010

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  This is the Dagster-vs-CLI resume tradeoff from earlier, now live: Dagster's
  predictions asset re-runs all 50 from the top (no resume) — re-materializing
  would re-spend the request on the 10 already done and could clobber them on
  the next 503. The CLI persists per-chunk and takes --offset, so you resume
  from P011, keeping P001–P010.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  When the model's responsive again (the 503 is intermittent — it cleared for
  chunk 1), resume with the CLI:
  uv run python src/main.py --offset=10     # P011–P050 (4 chunks); keeps 
  P001–P010
  If it 503s on a later chunk, the completed chunks stay — just bump the offset
  (--offset=20, 30, …) to continue from where it stopped. Don't re-Materialize 
  in Dagster for the extraction step (redoes all 50). Once all 50 are
  new-schema, render the downstream directly (no API):
  uv run quarto render README.qmd
  uv run python src/referral_pathway_analysis.py

  Where this leaves things

  - Quota: failed 503s still count, and today's had several attempts (08:43,
  09:15, 09:45, plus 12:13's chunk 1 success + chunk 2 fail). Pace the resume;
  if you start seeing 429 instead of 503, that's the daily cap — stop until
  reset.
  - Tests right now (mixed data): test_q3 may now pass (the 10 new files have
  biologic_timing=="BEFORE" records), but test_outputs still fails (the 40 old
  files have scalar reasons). Both go fully green only once all 50 are
  regenerated.
  - The new schema is proven — chunk 1 validated cleanly with list reasons,
  biologic_timing, lowercase classes. The only thing between you and a clean
  finish is Gemini's 503 spike clearing for the remaining 4 chunks.

⏺ Update(README.qmd)
  ⎿  Added 1 line, removed 1 line
      27  **Answers.**
      28  
      29  - **Enums vs. free text vs. objects.** Closed vocabularies the 
          analysis groups on are **enums** — `TreatmentOutcome`, `ReasonP
          rescribed`, `ReasonNotTaken`, `ComorbidCondition`, `PathwayStep
          ` — so `value_counts`/joins get stable categories and membershi
          p is checked at the model boundary (`enforce_validation`) — **G
          emini literally cannot return a value outside each enum, regard
          less of what the prompt lists** (the schema is sent as `respons
          e_schema` and the reply is validated, raising `JSONSchemaValida
          tionError` on a mismatch). Open, high-cardinality, not-yet-cano
          nical fields stay **free text**: `biologic_type`, `TreatmentRec
          ord.name`/`treatment_class` (a branded-drug + treatment-class r
          egistry is future work — `docs/TODO.md`). Repeated, multi-attri
          bute things are **structured objects/lists**: `treatment_record
          s` (a list of `TreatmentRecord`, each carrying `biologic_timing
          `, the field that answers Q3), `demographics`, and `referral_pa
          thway` (`list[PathwayStep]`). The visible cost of the free-text
           choice shows up in the **Q3 table** — `conventional_therapy`/`
          conventional` and case variants are one class left un-normalise
          d.
      30 -- **Three states, not one null.** "Not mentioned" (absence), "e
         -xplicitly denied" (negation) and "cut off" (truncation) are kep
         -t as *separate* signals rather than collapsed into a null: abse
         -nce → `biologic_not_mentioned` + null/empty fields + `NOT_MENTI
         -ONED`; negation → the `biologic_prescribed` vs. `biologic_taken
         -` pair + one or more `ReasonNotTaken` (`EXPLICIT_DENIAL`/`INSUR
         -ANCE_PROBLEMS`/…; multi-select); truncation → `churn` (a cut-of
         -f journey is the same truncation signal `churn` already carries
         -, so there is no separate `JOURNEY_CUT_OFF` reason — it was fol
         -ded into `churn`, like `incomplete_journey` before it). A `NOT_
         -APPLICABLE` member marks "genuinely doesn't apply" so it isn't 
         -confused with a blank "not yet annotated" cell. This is the spl
         -it the churn table under Q4 operationalises.                   
      30 +- **Three states, not one null.** "Not mentioned" (absence), "e
         +xplicitly denied" (negation) and "cut off" (truncation) are kep
         +t as *separate* signals rather than collapsed into a null: abse
         +nce → `biologic_not_mentioned` + null/empty fields + `NOT_MENTI
         +ONED`; negation → the `biologic_prescribed` vs. `biologic_taken
         +` pair + one or more `ReasonNotTaken` (`EXPLICIT_DENIAL`/`INSUR
         +ANCE_PROBLEMS`/…; multi-select); truncation → `churn` (a cut-of
         +f journey is the same truncation signal `churn` already carries
         +, so there is no separate `JOURNEY_CUT_OFF` reason — it was fol
         +ded into `churn`, like `incomplete_journey` before it). Explici
         +t non-reason values (`BIOLOGIC_TAKEN` when a biologic was taken
         +, `NOT_APPLICABLE` when none was ever prescribed) keep `reasons
         +_for_biologic_not_taken` **never empty**, so "doesn't apply" is
         + never confused with a missing value or a blank "not yet annota
         +ted" cell. This is the split the churn table under Q4 operation
         +alises.                                                        
      31  - **Reason enums — aligned to the spec, departing only where th
          e evidence demanded.** The spec's Q2 reasons (*doctor choice / 
          patient fears / cost / access*) shaped these enums, but reviewi
          ng all 50 transcripts drove two deliberate calls (evidenced in 
          [`docs/SCHEMA.md`](docs/SCHEMA.md) v0.7): **(1)** we keep `INSU
          RANCE_PROBLEMS` rather than the spec's bare *"access"* — every 
          real barrier in the data is payer-gating (step-therapy / prior-
          auth / tier), not drug *availability*, and `ACCESS` had **zero*
          * transcript support (covered by `COST` + `INSURANCE_PROBLEMS`)
          ; **(2)** `reasons_for_biologic_not_taken` is **multi-select** 
          (a list), because patients routinely have several reasons at on
          ce (e.g. cost + fear). Relatedly, `ReasonPrescribed` was trimme
          d to the initiation signal (`DOCTOR_CHOICE` / `PATIENT_REQUEST`
          ), since cost / fears / access are reasons a biologic is *not t
          aken*, not reasons it was prescribed.
      32  - **Evidence / auditability.** The model **does populate `evide
          nce_notes`** — a free-text rationale per patient (all 50 predic
          tions carry one, e.g. *"cycled through Remicade, Humira due to 
          loss of response; currently on Stelara; injection anxiety"*). W
          hat's **deferred is the manual side**: the gold annotation keep
          s `evidence_notes` as an unfilled column (`docs/SCHEMA.md`), an
          d structured **per-field** evidence — a snippet/turn-ref *per f
          ield* rather than one blob — is stretch task 2 (it costs tokens
           + prompt complexity). The broader audit trail is the **logged 
          raw model output** (`logs/extract.log` keeps the system prompt,
           schema, input and pre-validation response per call) plus the *
          *gold-set comparison** (`docs/TO_REVIEW.md`).
      33  - **Socio-demographics.** Deliberately minimal — `Demographics{
          gender, age}`, self-reported, grouped so a later "do pathways/o
          utcomes differ by demographic" cut is possible; everything else
           is noise for these four questions.

      209  
      210 -> **`unspecified` (Q2) — null handling.** An `unspecified` row
          - is a biologic **prescribed but not taken** with an **empty `r
          -easons_for_biologic_not_taken`** list (`[]`) — no reason extra
          -cted, not a stated one. It's kept **visible as its own categor
          -y** rather than dropped — consistent with the schema's stance 
          -on nulls (*Pydantic Schema Design → "Three states, not one nul
          -l"*): absence, negation and truncation are distinct first-clas
          -s states, and `UNKNOWN` / `NOT_APPLICABLE` exist so a missing 
          -value is never silently conflated with a real one. `system.txt
          -` now tells the model to always include a reason (default `[UN
          -KNOWN]`), so this should shrink on the next extraction run.   
      210 +> **`unspecified` (Q2) — null handling.** An `unspecified` row
          + is a biologic **prescribed but not taken** with an **empty `r
          +easons_for_biologic_not_taken`** list (`[]`) — no reason extra
          +cted, not a stated one. It's kept **visible as its own categor
          +y** rather than dropped — consistent with the schema's stance 
          +on nulls (*Pydantic Schema Design → "Three states, not one nul
          +l"*): absence, negation and truncation are distinct first-clas
          +s states, and `UNKNOWN` / `NOT_APPLICABLE` exist so a missing 
          +value is never silently conflated with a real one. `system.txt
          +` now requires a **never-empty** list (`[UNKNOWN]` for prescri
          +bed-but-not-taken with no stated reason, `[BIOLOGIC_TAKEN]` wh
          +en a biologic was taken), so `unspecified` should disappear on
          + the next extraction run.

⏺ Fair challenge — that was me being overly literal about your "~21 to_review
  cases" wording, and it's actually the wrong call. Let me explain and fix.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  BIOLOGIC_TAKEN is a logical consequence of biologic_taken == 1 — a patient who
  took a biologic has no "not taken" reason, regardless of whether a human 
  formally reviewed them. The to_review flag is about review provenance, not
  about whether the derivation is valid. So gating on to_review was wrong: it
  left P019 with biologic_taken=1 but reasons_for_biologic_not_taken = NaN —
  exactly the kind of untracked inconsistency we're trying to eliminate.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  There's a deeper oddity it exposed: P019 is to_review=0 yet has 
  biologic_taken=1 — an unreviewed row that already carries a gold annotation
  (and docs/TO_REVIEW.md actually discusses P019 as reviewed). So the data
  itself is inconsistent. I'll include P019 in the derivation now (the correct,
  consistent rule) and flag the to_review anomaly separately:

  Pacing (your earlier question) — and an important catch

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The BIOLOGIC_TAKEN change just made the already-regenerated P001–P010 stale 
  again — they were extracted with the previous prompt, so taken patients got []
  instead of [BIOLOGIC_TAKEN]. So don't resume with --offset=10 — that would
  leave P001–P010 inconsistent. The right move is one full run of all 50 once
  the schema/prompt is final. Every schema iteration invalidates partial data,
  so settle the design first, then run once.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Concrete pacing for that full run:
  - 5 chunks (10/chunk). Each chunk = 1 request (success) or up to 3 (1 + 2× 503
  retry).
  - Don't tight-loop a 503 — the spike outlasts the in-run 40s retry. If a chunk
  fails, wait ~10–15 min, then re-run; the CLI persists per-chunk and --offset
  resumes from the last good chunk.
  - If you see 429 (not 503), stop — that's the daily cap; resume after the
  Pacific-midnight reset.

  the Dagster predictions asset runs all 50 with no offset and no RetryPolicy.
  My claim holds, with one sharpening the adversarial pass surfaced:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  CLI "resume" is manual, not automatic — run_chunked never inspects data/out,
  so --offset resumes only because you point it at a later slice; re-running the
  same offset re-extracts and overwrites those patients. So the precise
  statement is: the CLI lets you resume by passing a larger --offset; Dagster 
  re-materialize has no resume path and redoes all 50.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  ✅ P019 inconsistency → documented as a TODO next-step

  The tell that the data is behind the code: P001 (a biologic-taken patient)

  _v7/BIOLOGIC_TAKEN work. The code is done; the data just needs one clean full
  run to catch up.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Pacing — the 503 is worse today, not better

  At 12:13 chunk 1 succeeded; by 12:55 chunk 1 fails outright (all 3 attempts
  503). Gemini's gemini-2.5-flash-lite has been under sustained "high demand"
  since ~08:43 today. Implications:
  - Each Dagster attempt costs ~3 requests (1 + 2 retries) on chunk 1 alone, and
  re-materialize always restarts at chunk 1 — so repeated tries burn quota with
  nothing to show.
  - Don't tight-loop. Wait for a genuinely quiet window — given it's been flaky
  for ~4h, that may mean an hour+ or an off-peak time, not minutes. The in-run
  40s retry clearly isn't outlasting these spikes.
  - Watch the error code: still 503 = Google capacity (your quota is fine). If
  it flips to 429, that's your daily cap — stop until the Pacific-midnight
  reset.
  - If you want a single run to ride out longer spikes, bump retry_503_delay_s
  (20 → 60) and/or retry_503_max in config.json — but each extra retry is still
  one more request, so it trades quota for resilience.

  Net: nothing regressed (this run wrote nothing; data/out is exactly as it was
  at 12:13). The only thing outstanding is one clean full extraction when the 
  model cooperates — that's what makes the BIOLOGIC_TAKEN/_v7 schema real in the
  data and clears the last test_outputs failure. Want me to bump the retry
  config for more resilience, or leave it frugal and wait for a quiet window?

  RETRY_503_DELAY_S`s apart.
  - DEBUGGING_ERROR_HANDLING.md (static): points to config.json instead of
  stating (2)/(20s).

  Gate: ruff/format/ty ✅; only the accepted test_outputs (stale data/out) still
  red.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The quota trade-off this buys: because run_chunked aborts on the first failed
  chunk, a fully-503'd run now costs ~5 requests (chunk 1: 1 + 4 retries) vs 3
  before — so ~4 fully-failed runs now ≈ the ~20/day cap (was ~6). But that's
  the point: the 4-minute in-run window waits the spike out itself, so you
  should re-run far less often — let one invocation ride it out rather than
  hammering. If it still gives up, that's a genuinely long outage, and waiting
  for an off-peak window beats spending more requests.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Practical guidance for the next attempt: kick off one run and let the 4-min
  retries do the waiting. If it still 503s out, wait for a clearly quieter
  window (it's been flaky for ~5h today). And the trip-wire is unchanged: if you
  ever see 429 instead of 503, stop — that's the daily cap, not capacity.

  The README's retry line + the docsite will reflect the new values on the next
  quarto render / mkdocs build (the render is still parked behind the one full
  extraction run that makes data/out consistent). Ready to commit whenever.

  The 429/503 decision is now in the code, not your head

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The fix is a single classifier, give_up_reason(e), that encodes the action for
  each terminal error — and both call sites use it, so the same decision shows
  up wherever the failure surfaces:
  - 429 → "NOT retried; STOP, re-running won't help until the quota resets"
  - 503 → "retried 4x then gave up; wait and re-run later" (pulls the live
  RETRY_503_MAX)

  This also fixes a latent inaccuracy: the old log said "gave up after retries"
  for every terminal error — but a 429 is never retried. Now the message tells
  the truth and tells you what to do, in both the CLI exit (main.py) and the
  logs (extract.py, which is what you read in a Dagster run). No
  log-interpreting required.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  What changed

  - src/extract.py — added give_up_reason(); both except HANDLED blocks now log
  the action-oriented reason instead of the misleading "gave up after retries".
  - src/main.py — the CLI exit routes through give_up_reason() (429 still
  appends the rate-limits URL); collapsed the old 4-branch handler.
  - README.qmd — the retry section now states the in-code decision concisely:
  "the stop-vs-retry decision is in the code (give_up_reason), not manual 
  log-watching — 429 → stop, 503 → wait and re-run" (retry counts stay dynamic
  via {python} RETRY_503_MAX).
  - tests/test_extract.py — 2 new offline reliability tests (429 → "STOP", 503 →
  "re-run") — the LLM-adjacent reliability check the brief asks for, and a
  guard against anyone making 429 look retryable.

  Verification (ultracode)

  Rather than a workflow for a 6-line pure classifier, I verified it directly 
  and permanently: a functional check confirmed all four error types map to the
  right action (429 STOP / 503 wait / connection / schema), and the two new
  pytest cases lock that behavior in so it can't silently regress.

  Net: the operator no longer watches logs to decide stop-vs-retry — the run
  does it and says so. Ready to commit. (README's new wording lands in README.md
  on the next quarto render, still parked behind the one full extraction run.)

  Why it still failed: this is 100% Google-side

  gemini-2.5-flash-lite has been "experiencing high demand" for ~5 hours today.
  The handling rode out a 4½-minute window in one shot — but the spike is simply
  longer than any reasonable in-run wait. No code change can fix a model that's
  down; it needs Google's capacity to recover. (And it's still 503, not 429 —
  so your quota isn't the blocker, though note each failed run now costs ~5
  requests, so watch for a 429 flip.)

  What can actually get you through

  1. Swap the model tier. The outage is specific to -lite. Try the fuller tier —
  it's a one-flag change (no code edit needed):
  uv run python src/main.py --model=gemini/gemini-2.5-flash
  1. …or set "model" in config.json. gemini-2.5-flash often has separate
  capacity from -lite; it may sail through where -lite is jammed. (It costs a
  bit more per token, but you've got 50 short transcripts.)
  2. Wait for off-peak. It's been flaky all day; Gemini free-tier load typically
  eases late evening / overnight Pacific. One clean run is all you need.
  3. Don't keep re-materializing now — each attempt burns ~5 requests on chunk 1
  and Dagster restarts from the top, so repeated tries just spend quota into a
  wall.

  Want me to switch config.json to gemini-2.5-flash so the next run uses the
  fuller tier (and bump it back to -lite after the successful extraction), or
  leave it and wait for the spike to pass?

⏺ The model swap worked past the 503 — gemini-2.5-flash responded! But the
  response was truncated (the JSON cuts off mid-array: "misdiagnosis",, then
  nothing), so enforce_validation rejected it → JSONSchemaValidationError → the
  strict chunk failed the run. Two real issues: (1) the truncation root cause,
  (2) one bad chunk shouldn't kill the whole pipeline. Let me diagnose the token
  usage first:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

❯ also what made that a JSONSchemaValidationError?                              

⏺ Diagnosis is clear now — and it's not a max-tokens-too-small-for-10-patients
  issue:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The logged rejected response is literally 1 character: {. The model produced
  almost no output before stopping. That's the signature of a reasoning model 
  eating its own token budget: gemini-2.5-flash thinks (unlike -lite, which
  barely does), and with max_tokens=8192 the thinking tokens consumed the whole 
  budget, leaving ~nothing for the JSON. Across attempts it varies — sometimes
  {, sometimes part-way through P001 (your pasted error) — exactly what a
  variable thinking budget does.

  What made it a JSONSchemaValidationError: we pass response_format={..., 
  "enforce_validation": True}, so LiteLLM validates the model's output against 
  response_schema inside the completion() call. The truncated { isn't valid JSON
  conforming to the schema → LiteLLM raises JSONSchemaValidationError (it's
  LiteLLM's enforce step, not our model_validate_json).
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Token usage: ~322k tokens / $0.08 cumulative across 25 logged calls (the whole
  log history, not just today) — trivial cost; quota's not the concern. The
  failed flash call burned thinking tokens but logged no telemetry (it raised
  before the usage line).

  Now both fixes — (1) give the output room so it stops truncating, (2)
  skip-and-log so one bad chunk never aborts the run:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

      38 +    """Run extract_batch over chunks of `chunk_size` — fewer Ge
         +mini requests for the daily cap.                               
      39 +                                                               
      40 +    A chunk whose output is invalid/truncated (`JSONSchemaValid
         +ationError` / Pydantic                                         
      41 +    `ValidationError`) is logged and **skipped** so one bad chu
         +nk can't abort the whole run; the                              
      42 +    skipped patients are counted + logged. Quota/overload error
         +s (429/503) still propagate to stop                            
      43 +    the run, since continuing is futile (see `give_up_reason`).
         +"""                                                            
      44      out: list[PatientLabels] = []
      45 +    skipped: list[str] = []                                    
      46      for i in range(0, len(records), chunk_size):
      41 -        out += extract_batch(records[i : i + chunk_size], out_d
         -ir, model)                                                     
      47 +        chunk = records[i : i + chunk_size]                    
      48 +        try:                                                   
      49 +            out += extract_batch(chunk, out_dir, model)        
      50 +        except (litellm.JSONSchemaValidationError, ValidationEr
         +ror):                                                          
      51 +            ids = [r.patient_id for r in chunk]                
      52 +            skipped += ids                                     
      53 +            logger.error("chunk {} skipped (invalid/truncated o
         +utput) — continuing run", ids)                                 
      54 +    if skipped:                                                
      55 +        logger.warning(                                        
      56 +            "run_chunked: {}/{} patients skipped after extracti
         +on failures: {}",                                              
      57 +            len(skipped),                                      
      58 +            len(records),                                      
      59 +            skipped,                                           
      60 +        )                                                      
      61      return out

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The docsite-ordering workflow's verdict: it's not an ordering bug — it's a 
  freshness gap. docs/referral_pathways.md is a committed, pre-rendered Quarto
  artefact (referral_pathways.qmd loops pathway_mermaid() over data/out/json).

  way). The defect is that no asset regenerates the .md from new predictions, so
  the committed gallery silently drifts (it's stale right now: committed P001

      33  | `ServiceUnavailableError` (**503**) | model busy / transient 
          overload | `_with_503_retry` retries **sparingly** — `RETRY_503
          _MAX` attempts, `RETRY_503_DELAY_S` apart (both set in `config.
          json`) — then a concise `ERROR` line (no stack dump — `excluded
          ` from `@logger.catch`) and re-raise. litellm's own retries are
           OFF (`num_retries=0`). | `src/extract.py` |
      34  | `RateLimitError` (**429**) | rate-limit / daily quota | **NOT
           retried — fails fast.** On the free tier a failed request cost
          s the same daily quota as a successful one, so retrying a quota
           error just burns more of it. Concise `ERROR` line, then re-rai
          sed. | `src/extract.py` |
      35  | 429 (or exhausted 503) reaching the CLI | quota hit, or 503 r
          etries used up | `main()` catches it and **exits gracefully** —
           a clean one-liner with the rate-limits URL, exit 1, no traceba
          ck (fail-fast) | `src/main.py` |
      36 +| `JSONSchemaValidationError` / Pydantic `ValidationError` | ou
         +tput invalid/truncated (e.g. a reasoning model exhausts `max_to
         +kens` before finishing the JSON) | **Skip that chunk + log, con
         +tinue the run** — one bad chunk can't abort the whole extractio
         +n; skipped `patient_id`s are counted + logged. `enforce_validat
         +ion` raises this *inside* `litellm.completion`. | `src/main.py`
         + (`run_chunked`) |

⏺ All green — ruff/format/ty pass, pipeline.py loads with the new asset (assets:
  loaded ok), 18 passed, and only the accepted test_outputs stale-data fail
  remains. Here's the full picture across everything that converged:

  1. Why it truncated (token diagnosis)

  gemini-2.5-flash is a reasoning model; its thinking tokens count against
  max_tokens, and 8192 left ~nothing for the JSON → output cut off (sometimes
  mid-referral_pathway, sometimes at just {). Fix: max_tokens 8192 → 32768 in
  config.json (with a _comment_max_tokens explaining why), so thinking and a
  10-patient batch both fit.

  2. Skip-and-log instead of aborting (your main ask)

  run_chunked (src/main.py) now wraps each chunk:
  - Invalid/truncated output (JSONSchemaValidationError / Pydantic
  ValidationError) → log the chunk's patient_ids, skip, continue. At the end it
  logs N/total patients skipped.
  - 429 / 503 → still propagate and stop (continuing is futile —
  give_up_reason).

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  A nice emergent property: because predictions now succeeds with a partial list
  instead of raising, the whole downstream DAG still completes on a partial run
  — referral_graphs, readme, referral_pathways_md, docsite all render from
  whatever extracted. One bad chunk no longer costs you the entire pipeline
  output.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  3. The docsite "ordering" question → it was a freshness gap, now fixed

  Not an ordering bug (mkdocs copies the pre-rendered .md), but the .md was
  never regenerated from new predictions. Wired the real dependency in
  src/pipeline.py:
  predictions ─→ referral_pathways_md  (quarto render
  docs/referral_pathways.qmd)
                        └─→ docsite      (mkdocs build)
  Now the journey gallery is rebuilt from the latest predictions before mkdocs 
  copies it — exactly your "it should follow the API calls" intuition.

  What refreshes on the next successful run

  Both stale artefacts clear once a clean extraction lands (now unblocked:
  -flash for the 503, max_tokens for the truncation, skip-and-log as the safety
  net):
  - the 40 old data/out/*.json → clears test_outputs,
  - the stale docs/referral_pathways.md (committed P001 = 15 nodes vs
  current 12) → auto-rebuilt via the new asset.

  Files changed (ready for your commit): config.json, src/main.py,
  src/pipeline.py, docs/DEBUGGING_ERROR_HANDLING.md.

  One optional optimization: since extraction is pure classification, it doesn't
  need flash's reasoning at all — I can add reasoning_effort="disable" (LiteLLM
  → Gemini thinkingBudget=0) to the completion calls, which would eliminate the
  truncation cause entirely and cut cost/latency (no wasted thinking tokens).
  Want me to add that, or leave the headroom approach as-is and re-run first?

⏺ Good question to pin down — they're not redundant, they solve different
  layers:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  - #1 (non-reasoning) fixes the cause — it stops the thinking step from eating
  the token budget, so chunks don't truncate in the first place. It's a
  quality/cost/determinism win for what is fundamentally a classification task
  (no chain-of-thought needed — -lite proved that by working with ~no thinking).
  - #2 (skip-and-log) is a safety net for the unknown — any chunk that still
  fails for any reason (a one-off bad generation, a schema edge case, a future
  model swap) gets skipped + logged instead of killing all 50.

  So: #1 makes failures rare; #2 makes a rare failure non-fatal. The brief's
  robustness section explicitly wants #2 regardless, so I'd keep #2 (already
  done) and add #1 as the proper root-cause fix. max_tokens=32768 then just
  becomes harmless headroom.

⏺ Update(README.qmd)
  ⎿  Added 1 line, removed 1 line
      55  
      56  - **Single-shot, batched — not multi-stage.** One system prompt
           extracts *all* `PatientLabels` fields in a single structured c
          all per patient; transcripts are then **batched** (10 per reque
          st) only to fit Gemini's free-tier ~20-requests/day cap — batch
          ing ≠ staging, it's still one pass over every field, just sever
          al patients per call. Single-shot minimises calls (decisive und
          er the rate cap), keeps the code simple, and lets the model set
           inter-dependent fields (`biologic_prescribed`/`biologic_taken`
          , `churn`, `referral_pathway`) with the whole story in view. Th
          e cost: every field competes for attention in one prompt, so th
          e hard fields suffer — the EDA shows `churn` unreliable and the
           GP node under-emitted. A multi-stage split (per-section prompt
          s run concurrently, with inspectable intermediates) is the #1 i
          tem in *Next Steps* below; it trades more calls + orchestration
           for better per-field accuracy.
      57  - **Uncertainty: absence vs. negation vs. truncation.** The pro
          mpt separates them by construction, onto the three schema mecha
          nisms above. A global rule — *"Leave a field null or empty when
           the transcript does not support a confident value"* — covers *
          *absence**. **Negation** rides on the prescribed-vs-taken boole
          ans + the `ReasonNotTaken` enum. **Truncation** gets the most e
          xplicit instruction: judge `churn` from how the transcript *end
          s* — churn **only** when it breaks off mid-word / mid-thought (
          e.g. ends on "Chronic…"), while a deliberately-typed "…" on a c
          oherent phrase is an intentional sign-off, **not** churn (no re
          currence heuristic) — and — critically — *"keep this truncation
           signal distinct from a topic simply being absent … (that is 'n
          ot mentioned', not churn)."* Honest limit: truncation is a lexi
          cal/structural property of the transcript's end that the narrat
          ive under-determines, so the instruction alone isn't enough — t
          he gold audit found it unreliable in both directions (over-flag
          ging and missed truncation), and a deterministic tail-of-text r
          ule would do better (`docs/TO_REVIEW.md`).
      58 -- **Determinism & reproducibility.** Three levers, in decreasin
         -g order of leverage. **(1) Structured-output mode** — `response
         -_format={"type": "json_object", "response_schema": PatientLabel
         -s.model_json_schema(), "enforce_validation": true}`: a Pydantic
         --derived schema constrains the *shape* and enum membership, and
         - LiteLLM validates the reply (raising `JSONSchemaValidationErro
         -r` on a mismatch), so format/parse variance is removed and down
         -stream pandas never meets an off-vocabulary value. **(2) Persis
         -ted predictions** — each chunk writes one validated JSON per pa
         -tient to `data/out/`, and the whole analysis (this README) read
         -s *only* those frozen artefacts with no API call, so every numb
         -er and plot is **exactly reproducible run-to-run** regardless o
         -f LLM nondeterminism; this is the real guarantee. **(3) Decodin
         -g params** — `temperature` is **pinned to `0`** (greedy) in `co
         -nfig.json` and imported by `extract.py` — extraction here is a 
         -**classification** task, so we want *same transcript → same lab
         -els*, not sampled variety. This build is the live test case for
         - *why*: a fresh re-run at the provider default (Gemini's defaul
         -t `temperature` is `1.0`) shifted long-tail outputs (the GP-nod
         -e count; which biologics got mislabelled on `biologic_timing`) 
         -while the headline answers held — exactly the run-to-run drift 
         -`0` removes. Temperature is **logged per run** (with the model)
         - so runs at different parameter levels stay comparable. A seed 
         -would add belt-and-braces, but with a reasoning-capable model `
         -0` *minimises* rather than *guarantees* bit-identical output; *
         -*(2)** is the hard reproducibility guarantee for the analysis. 
         -No explicit prompt **caching** yet — the static system prompt +
         - schema are identical across calls, so implicit context caching
         - may apply, and cached-token counts are logged (`docs/TELEMETRY
         -.md`).

⏺ All green, and reasoning_effort="disable" confirmed mapping to
  thinkingBudget=0 on the live Gemini path. Now your CoT question — it's the
  most important conceptual point here, and the stretch list answers it
  precisely.

  Why CoT isn't needed for this call — and what the brief actually means by it

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The stretch idea (TASK_INSTRUCTIONS.md:148) is: "Multi-stage or 
  chain-of-thought prompts with inspectable intermediate artifacts." That
  qualifier is everything. There are two different things both called
  "chain-of-thought":

  1. Hidden internal thinking (thinkingBudget — what we just disabled). Opaque
  reasoning tokens the model emits before the answer. includeThoughts=False →
  not in the output, not auditable, not reproducible. For single-shot extraction
  it added run-to-run variance, burned the token budget (→ the truncation), and
  bought no accuracy.
  2. Explicit multi-stage / CoT (the stretch idea). Reasoning made into visible,
  auditable artifacts — e.g. identify-then-extract or narrative→structured
  stages, each inspectable and eval-able. The value is auditability + per-field
  accuracy, delivered by architecture, not hidden tokens.

  These are almost opposites: #1 hides reasoning inside one call; #2 surfaces it
  across stages. Disabling #1 is consistent with the brief's stated values —
  "trustworthy structured data," evidence-per-field, reproducibility — while #2
  is the thing it rewards.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Why this task doesn't need #1: it's span-extraction + closed-vocabulary
  classification against a schema that enforce_validation already polices —
  there's no multi-step logical/arithmetic chain to think through. The README
  itself diagnoses the real weakness: "every field competes for attention in one
  prompt, so the hard fields suffer — churn unreliable, GP node under-emitted."
  That's an attention-competition problem, not a lack-of-reasoning problem —
  more internal thinking won't fix it. And churn is unreliable for a structural
  reason (it's a lexical property of the transcript's end); a deterministic
  tail-of-text rule beats any amount of reasoning there.

  Where CoT genuinely earns its place here: the #1 Next Step — splitting into
  focused, concurrent stages with inspectable intermediates. That's exactly #2:
  it fixes the attention-competition (better
  churn/referral_pathway/biologic_timing) and gives a reviewer auditable
  artifacts — which is the brief's whole point. So: turn off the opaque thinking
  now; add explicit, inspectable CoT later. Complementary, not contradictory.

  What's now wired (this turn)

  - reasoning_effort="disable" on both completion calls (extract +
  extract_batch); safe via the existing drop_params=True (dropped on the Ollama
  fallback). Verified → thinkingConfig.thinkingBudget=0.
  - extract_batch docstring corrected — no longer "strict, fails the whole
  chunk"; now points to run_chunked skip-and-log.
  - README determinism section documents the disable + the verified mapping +
  the CoT distinction above.
  - config.json _comment_max_tokens refreshed (thinking no longer competes for
  the budget).

  Decision recap on your two questions

  1. Non-reasoning version — yes, done. Root-cause fix (removes truncation +
  variance + cost), correct for a classification task.
  2. Skip-on-validation-failure — yes, keep (done). Independent safety net for
  any future chunk failure; the brief's robustness ask.

  Gate: ruff/format/ty clean, 18 passed, only the accepted test_outputs
  stale-data fail. Pipeline DAG audited correct — all EDA/plots/graphs/mermaid
  rebuild from fresh predictions.

  Changes ready for your commit: src/extract.py, src/main.py, src/pipeline.py,
  config.json, README.qmd, docs/DEBUGGING_ERROR_HANDLING.md. With -flash
  (separate 503 pool), max_tokens=32768, reasoning off, and skip-and-log as
  backstop, the next run should sail through.

⏺ Found it — it's a chunk-ordering bug, not data:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  GOLD_FILE is defined at line 121 (GOLD_FILE = GOLD.name), but it's used inline
  at line 72 ({python} GOLD_FILE) — 49 lines earlier. Quarto evaluates code
  cells and inline {python} expressions in document order against one shared
  kernel, so when the line-72 inline runs, GOLD_FILE doesn't exist yet →
  NameError. The fix is to define it in the first setup chunk, before any use.
  (This only surfaced now because the render finally got far enough to execute
  that inline — the prior blocker was upstream.)
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

⏺ Excellent — this is a failure caused by the model getting better:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The fresh -flash run now flags P049 churn=True — the exact truncation case it
  used to miss. So churn_false_negatives() returns [] (zero false negatives on
  the gold churn set), and the test's hard-coded assert "P049" in fn fails
  because the model improved. churn detection — the field we'd flagged as the
  weakest link — measurably got better with the bigger model + disabled
  reasoning.

  The lesson: that assertion conflated the function's logic with a specific 
  run's result. The run result is dynamic (and now better); the function logic
  is what a unit test should pin. Let me rewrite it to test the logic
  deterministically against the real gold anchor (P049), independent of which
  way the live model happened to call it.

❯ what does this mean?  (with churn being the known-unreliable field            
⏺ First, your question — "with churn being the known-unreliable field":

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  churn is the schema field that marks whether a patient's interview was cut off
  mid-journey (they disengaged) versus ended naturally. Across this project's
  gold audit (docs/TO_REVIEW.md) it's been the least trustworthy extracted field
  — wrong in both directions (flagging churn that wasn't there, and missing
  real truncation). The reason is structural: truncation is a property of how 
  the transcript ends (a lexical/structural signal), which the narrative content
  under-determines — so the model has to guess from the ending, and the README
  notes a deterministic tail-of-text rule would beat it.

  So when I said the churn test would be "the one to shift," I meant: of all
  fields, churn is the most run-to-run variable precisely because it's the least
  reliable — so changing the model (-lite → -flash) was always most likely to
  move its predictions. And it did: for the better. churn was the field most
  likely to break a hard-coded test, which is exactly what happened.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Now the fix — rewrite the test to pin the function's logic (deterministic,
  against the real gold anchor P049), not a specific run's result:

      58  ## Architecture notes
      59  
      60  - **LLM access pattern**: go through `litellm.completion(model=
          "gemini/...", ...)`, not the raw `google-genai` SDK. The SDK is
           only present because it was used in the notebook to sanity-che
          ck the API key before LiteLLM was wired in. LiteLLM gives us `r
          esponse_format={"type": "json_object"}` and a `response_schema`
           hook for Gemini (with optional `enforce_validation: true`) — p
          refer that over hand-parsing model output.
      61 -- **Reproducibility**: `gemini-2.5-flash-lite` supports reasoni
         -ng (`litellm.supports_reasoning(...)` returns True) and caching
         -/TTL. If you add prompt caching or thinking-mode toggles, docum
         -ent them where they're set — they affect token accounting and r
         -un-to-run determinism.                                         
      61 +- **Reproducibility**: extraction is a classification task, so 
         +**reasoning is disabled** (`reasoning_effort="disable"` → `thin
         +kingBudget=0`, in `extract.py`) alongside `temperature=0`. `gem
         +ini-2.5-flash` supports reasoning/caching, but thinking is off 
         +to avoid run-to-run variance and output-budget truncation (see 
         +README → Determinism). Document any further caching/thinking to
         +ggles where they're set — they affect token accounting and dete
         +rminism.                                                       
      62  - **Data layout**: `data/in/` is committed input (`interviews.j
          son`), `data/out/` is for generated artefacts, `data/prompts/` 
          for prompt templates. Keep generated outputs out of `data/in/`.
      63  - **Notebooks**: jupytext-paired (`ipynb,py:percent`). **Edit n
          otebook code in the `.py`** (the editing source of truth → clea
          n diffs) and run `jupytext --sync`; **never hand-edit the `.ipy
          nb`** — that's the human's running/viewing copy, where outputs 
          live. `notebooks/` is excluded from the gate. Full workflow: `d
          ocs/DEV_SETUP.md`.

❯ what does this mean?  docsite labelled "independent",                         

❯ fix elsewhere in docs/scripts                                                 

⏺ Let me answer your two questions first, then sweep.

  "docsite labelled 'independent'" — In the original Dagster graph, the docsite
  asset had no deps, so Dagster treated it as independent of predictions — free
  to run before/parallel to the data. That's exactly the bug from earlier:
  mkdocs would copy the stale committed referral_pathways.md. The old diagram
  literally printed docsite … · independent to show that. Now that docsite
  depends on referral_pathways_md (which depends on predictions), it's no longer
  independent — so I removed that label. That's what the word meant.

  On the .ods version — in code it's already SSOT: config.json gold →
  config.GOLD, used everywhere, and the README renders the filename dynamically
  (GOLD_FILE). The _v6 the audit flagged is just version history in the SCHEMA
  changelog (correct to keep). The fix needed is the same SSOT principle applied
  to the static docs that restate the literal value.

  Applying your principle now — genericize CLAUDE.md + the diagram's model node
  to point at the SSOT, and read the other stale-prone docs/scripts:

      58  ## Architecture notes

      60  - **LLM access pattern**: go through `litellm.completion(model=
          "gemini/...", ...)`, not the raw `google-genai` SDK. The SDK is
           only present because it was used in the notebook to sanity-che
          ck the API key before LiteLLM was wired in. LiteLLM gives us `r
          esponse_format={"type": "json_object"}` and a `response_schema`
           hook for Gemini (with optional `enforce_validation: true`) — p
          refer that over hand-parsing model output.

         -ini-2.5-flash` supports reasoning/caching, but thinking is off 
         -to avoid run-to-run variance and output-budget truncation (see 
         -README → Determinism). Document any further caching/thinking to
         -ggles where they're set — they affect token accounting and dete
         -rminism.

          n diffs) and run `jupytext --sync`; **never hand-edit the `.ipy

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  Your rule is now both applied and self-documenting. The mechanism differs by
  doc type, but the principle is uniform:
  - .qmd (rendered) → render the value ({python} MODEL, GOLD_FILE, APP_VERSION)
  — it can never drift because it's read from the SSOT at build time.
  - .md (static) → link to the SSOT ([config.json](../config.json)) instead of
  restating the literal.
  - History (CHANGELOG, SCHEMA changelog, "ran on -lite during iteration") →
  naming old→new is correct, because it's a record of change, not a live
  pointer.

  I encoded the rule itself in CLAUDE.md ("don't restate a literal; link to its
  SSOT") so future edits follow it.

❯ how come this is v08 but the .ods latest is v07?  Ground-truth annotation     
  schema (v0.8)                                                                 

⏺ Good catch — that's two different version numbers, and it's worth being
  precise about why. Let me read SCHEMA.md to confirm exactly what v0.8 tracks
  vs. the _v7 data file:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Not a bug — they're two independent version counters for two different 
  artefacts, and they happen to correspond at the latest step:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  - v0.8 versions the schema definition — the vocabulary/fields documented in
  docs/SCHEMA.md (live source: src/schema.py). Its changelog counts schema
  changes: v0.4 (incomplete_journey→churn), v0.5 (BiologicTiming), v0.6 (drop
  JOURNEY_CUT_OFF), v0.7 (reason enums realigned), v0.8 (BIOLOGIC_TAKEN).
  - _v7 versions the gold data file — interviews_ground_truth_v7.ods
  (config.json gold), counting rebuilds by sandbox/build_ground_truth_v*.py: _v2
  → … → _v7.

  They're off by one in number but aligned in meaning: the v0.8 change
  (BIOLOGIC_TAKEN) is exactly what produced _v7.ods. The numbers differ only
  because the doc and the data file have been revised a different number of
  times (the schema doc started its count earlier). It's the same pattern as the
  app version (0.1.0, pyproject.toml) being a third, separate counter —
  definition, data, and app are distinct things, each single-sourced.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  The real fix is your own principle: it wasn't documented, so it read as an
  inconsistency. Let me make the relationship explicit in SCHEMA.md:

❯ and is this the first version of the schema? "not implemented yet" sounds     
  misleading given we have an updated working schema. it is okay if it is a     
  "first version" Toward a Pydantic schema                                      
  Target model (not implemented yet — recorded so annotation maps cleanly onto  
  code):                                                                        
                                                                                
  from enum import Enum                                                         
  from pydantic import BaseModel                                                
                                                                                
  class TreatmentOutcome(str, Enum):                                            
      SUCCESS = "SUCCESS"                                                       
      FAILED = "FAILED"                                                         
      PARTIAL = "PARTIAL"                                                       
      AMBIGUOUS = "AMBIGUOUS"   # stated but mixed / contradictory              
      ONGOING = "ONGOING"       # unresolved / still escalating                 
      UNKNOWN = "UNKNOWN"       # not stated                                    
                                                                                
  class ReasonPrescribed(str, Enum):   # initiation signal (single)             
      DOCTOR_CHOICE = "DOCTOR_CHOICE"                                           
      PATIENT_REQUEST = "PATIENT_REQUEST"                                       
      NOT_APPLICABLE = "NOT_APPLICABLE"                                         
      OTHER = "OTHER"                                                           
                                                                                
  class ReasonNotTaken(str, Enum):     # renamed from ReasonDenied; expanded    
      NOT_MENTIONED = "NOT_MENTIONED"                                           
      EXPLICIT_DENIAL = "EXPLICIT_DENIAL"                                       
      INSURANCE_PROBLEMS = "INSURANCE_PROBLEMS"                                 
      COST = "COST"                                                             
      PATIENT_FEARS = "PATIENT_FEARS"                                           
      CONTRAINDICATION = "CONTRAINDICATION"                                     
      DEFERRED = "DEFERRED"                                                     
      UNKNOWN = "UNKNOWN"                                                       
      BIOLOGIC_TAKEN = "BIOLOGIC_TAKEN"

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The whole docs/ tree is converging on one standard, which is really your two
  rules applied uniformly: (1) SSOT — every current value (model, paths,
  versions, params) is stated once (config.json / pyproject.toml /
  src/schema.py) and linked elsewhere; (2) DRY — no doc re-explains what another
  owns (DEV_SETUP links CLAUDE/README instead of repeating; SCHEMA points to
  the code instead of mirroring it); and changes go to CHANGELOG.md, not into
  the live pointers. That's why the audits keep finding the same shape of fix —
  it's one principle, many sites.

⏺ HYPOTHESES.md is excellent — the Actual column is filled from the run and
  every figure links to its source (README.md#q1…, data/out/json/,
  src/schema.py, config.json), with untested hypotheses kept honestly open.
  That's exactly the SSOT pattern.

  But the SSOT-linking surfaced a real tension: mkdocs now warns on ~25 links to
  repo-root files (../config.json, ../src/schema.py, ../CLAUDE.md,
  ../README.md) — they're valid on GitHub but live outside docs_dir, so the
  site's link-checker flags them.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  This is the dual-publish problem: these docs render both as the GitHub repo
  tree (where ../config.json resolves) and as the mkdocs API site (which only
  hosts docs/). The links are correct for the primary view (GitHub) and
  shouldn't be rewritten to hard-coded https://github.com/... URLs — that would
  re-introduce a drift-prone literal (the repo URL), violating the very SSOT
  rule we're applying. The right fix is to tell mkdocs these out-of-site links
  are intentional, so its check stops flagging false positives.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Let me read mkdocs.yml and add that validation setting:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  What started as "fix the model name" converged into one durable invariant
  across the repo: every current value is stated once at its source (config.json
  / pyproject.toml / src/schema.py) and linked everywhere else; every change is
  recorded in CHANGELOG.md; nothing is duplicated or restated. That's why the
  audits kept finding the same shape of fix — and why the rule now lives in
  CLAUDE.md, so the next person (or model) maintains it automatically. The one
  new wrinkle — mkdocs flagging those out-of-site links — is resolved at the
  config layer rather than by hard-coding URLs, keeping the repo portable.
